LLQP Answer Keys and Explanations

Numbered to match the practice tests in your printed study guide.

Practice Test 1 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (Level term coverage aligned precisely with the remaining twenty five year mortgage period): Daniel needs inexpensive protection for a defined temporary period that matches his mortgage obligation. Level term supplies that coverage without requiring the longer commitment or higher cost of permanent insurance. Whole life and universal life address lifelong or investment focused objectives that Daniel did not describe. Joint last to die coverage insures two lives and is designed for second death estate planning rather than one borrower's mortgage.
  2. The correct answer is D (Renewal premiums reflect the insured person's attained age on each renewal date): Renewable term guarantees another coverage period without new health evidence, but pricing is refreshed using the insured person's older age. Mortality risk generally rises with attained age, so the premium can increase sharply even when health has not changed. The original issue age does not freeze renewal prices throughout the contract. Regulator approved inflation formulas and automatic reductions tied to policy duration do not explain this pricing pattern.
  3. The correct answer is A (Participating or non participating whole life coverage with guaranteed values): Whole life provides permanent insurance, a level premium, and a guaranteed minimum cash value schedule that can support a predictable retirement need. Those features directly match her preference for contractual certainty over market linked outcomes. Renewable term has no cash value and cannot supplement retirement income. Equity indexed universal life introduces performance variability, while employer group term is temporary coverage lacking a guaranteed cash value schedule.
  4. The correct answer is B (Universal life coverage with flexible premiums and separately stated insurance charges): Universal life separates insurance costs and expenses from the accumulated fund, and its flexible premium design allows deposits to vary within contract limits. The account value can pay monthly deductions when the owner skips or reduces a planned deposit. Whole life generally obliges the owner to follow its scheduled premium. Renewability changes whether a term contract can continue, while employer funded group coverage does not create a personal flexible account for covering missed deposits.
  5. The correct answer is B (The contract may lapse after account value stops covering monthly charges): Universal life deductions are paid from the account value, so continued depletion without enough new premium can end the policy. A lapse risk is the practical consequence once the fund cannot satisfy the contractual charges. Paid up whole life is not an automatic fallback in a standard universal life contract. The insurer neither increases the benefit to absorb the shortfall nor moves the person into an employer group plan.
  6. The correct answer is C (Extended term or reduced paid up coverage uses available cash value): Non forfeiture provisions convert existing cash value into either continued term protection or a smaller permanent amount that requires no further premiums. This preserves insurance value rather than allowing the contract's accumulated value to disappear. Straight forfeiture defeats the purpose of these protective provisions. Segregated funds do not replace the policy's death benefit automatically, and an individual whole life policy does not move itself into group coverage.
  7. The correct answer is D (Children's term insurance coverage linked to the parent's permanent contract): A children's term rider adds modest temporary protection for eligible children under the parent's existing policy. It is inexpensive and tied directly to the family arrangement described. Waiver of premium protects the policyowner's premium obligation during disability rather than insuring a daughter. An accidental death benefit adds payment for an accident, and guaranteed insurability permits additional purchases on the father instead of child coverage.
  8. The correct answer is A (Guaranteed insurability coverage with scheduled future purchase opportunities): A guaranteed insurability rider gives the owner defined opportunities to buy additional coverage at stated dates or events without new health evidence. That structure protects against future insurability concerns tied to his family history. Accident coverage responds to specific accidental losses rather than future policy purchases. Conversion changes existing term coverage into a permanent plan, while a cost of living rider adjusts current coverage for inflation rather than granting scheduled purchase rights.
  9. The correct answer is D (Accidental death and dismemberment coverage with scheduled loss benefits): This rider adds payment for accidental death and specified accidental losses such as the loss of a limb or sight. It supplies the extra protection the client wants while the base policy continues. Waiver of premium helps maintain coverage during qualifying disability but does not pay scheduled dismemberment benefits. Guaranteed insurability supports future purchases, and conversion changes the policy structure rather than adding accident loss coverage.
  10. The correct answer is B (Waiver of premium coverage keeping the contract active during disability): A waiver of premium rider suspends the owner's premium obligation after the contractual waiting period when total disability meets the policy definition. The contract can therefore remain active without surrendering coverage. Children's term insures eligible dependents instead of addressing the owner's disability. Conversion changes the plan type, and guaranteed insurability creates future purchase rights rather than waiving current premiums.
  11. The correct answer is A (Group underwriting based on the overall characteristics of the covered employee group): Group life underwriting evaluates the workforce as a whole and commonly grants automatic coverage during initial eligibility up to a stated non evidence maximum. That approach allows eligible full time employees to enroll without individual medical examinations. Complete personal underwriting and separate personal rating classes would defeat the plan's streamlined group design. Age based mandatory paramedical examinations are not the standard basis for this enrollment process.
  12. The correct answer is C (Coverage usually ends after employment ends, subject to a conversion privilege): Active employment is the basis for group certificate eligibility, so leaving the employer normally ends that coverage. Many group contracts then allow conversion to an individual plan within a limited period, often without new evidence. The benefit does not continue for life at no cost after payroll deductions stop. It also does not transfer into another employer's contract or become paid up permanent insurance without the member taking the required action.
  13. The correct answer is A (Group experience rating from aggregate claims, size, and demographic composition): Experience rating reviews the covered group's claims record, scale, and demographic make up when setting renewal rates. The risk is assessed collectively rather than through a new medical rating for every certificate holder. Group term rates are reviewed and can change at renewal instead of staying fixed for the contract's entire life. Occupation may provide useful information, but one employee risk class does not explain a group rating based on multiple aggregate factors.
  14. The correct answer is C (Substandard or rated classification for insurable above average mortality risk): Controlled hypertension can place an applicant above average mortality risk while still allowing the insurer to issue coverage at an extra premium. That outcome is represented by a substandard or rated class. Preferred pricing recognizes better than average health and does not describe this case. A decline means no policy is issued, and standard pricing assumes average mortality rather than the additional cost shown here.
  15. The correct answer is B (Preferred or preferred plus classification reflecting better than average health): Several favorable indicators place this applicant above the average underwriting profile expected for standard pricing. Insurers commonly recognize that superior profile through a preferred or preferred plus rating class. Substandard and table rated classes increase cost for elevated mortality risk, which contradicts the stated health evidence. Standard pricing would overlook the stronger than average health profile that supports preferred treatment.
  16. The correct answer is D (Decline the request because the mortality risk exceeds acceptable limits): Once the risk remains too high even after considering an extra mortality charge, the insurer cannot reasonably issue the requested individual coverage. Declining the application is the appropriate underwriting result. Preferred and standard issue would ignore evidence showing unacceptable mortality risk. An individual application also cannot be shifted automatically into an employer group plan because group eligibility depends on a separate employment relationship and plan rules.
  17. The correct answer is B (Universal life coverage offering several investment account choices): Universal life can provide permanent protection while giving the owner choices among investment accounts inside the policy. Account value and certain death benefit designs can vary with the performance of those choices. Participating whole life relies on dividends declared by the insurer instead of owner directed account choices. Renewable term has no cash value investments, and basic group life does not give the certificate holder personal investment direction.
  18. The correct answer is C (They reflect divisible surplus, depend on experience, and are not guaranteed): Participating policy dividends reflect the insurer's favorable mortality, expense, and investment experience as divisible surplus. Because actual experience changes, the amount is not guaranteed from year to year. These payments belong to participating whole life arrangements rather than non participating universal life contracts. Owners commonly choose among cash, premium reduction, accumulation with interest, paid up additions, or one year term, so cash is not the sole available use.
  19. The correct answer is D (Early level premiums exceed early mortality costs and build supporting reserves): Whole life uses a level premium that is higher than the current mortality cost in the early policy years. The excess accumulates in reserves and cash value, helping fund the higher mortality costs expected at older ages without raising the premium. The insurer does not simply absorb later losses with no reserve mechanism. Mortality risk generally rises with age, and whole life premiums are not recalculated periodically like renewable term rates.
  20. The correct answer is A (The twenty year term starts higher because it locks mortality costs longer): A longer level premium period must account for the greater mortality cost expected during later covered years. The insurer therefore charges more from the start for the twenty year guarantee than for the ten year policy at the same face amount and rating. The premiums are not identical because the periods of price protection differ. Level premiums remain level during the guaranteed term instead of falling at year ten, and longer term coverage is not normally the cheaper initial option.
  21. The correct answer is D (A conversion privilege exercised within the contract's stated time limits): A term conversion privilege changes eligible term coverage into a permanent plan without new evidence of insurability. The owner must meet the contract's deadline and select an available permanent plan. Guaranteed insurability riders create set opportunities to buy additional coverage rather than convert the current policy. Waiver of premium addresses disability, and an automatic premium loan uses cash value to prevent a missed premium from lapsing the contract.
  22. The correct answer is A (It keeps the net amount at risk higher than a level benefit option): Under a face plus account value design, the total death benefit rises with the fund while the insurer's exposed amount stays closer to the original face amount. Compared with a level death benefit, that larger amount at risk can keep cost of insurance deductions higher as account value builds. The design does not reduce or remove mortality charges merely because the fund grows. It is a universal life benefit option rather than a term insurance feature.
  23. The correct answer is B (An attending physician statement and possibly additional medical tests): Two heart attacks are material cardiac findings that require clinical detail beyond basic application answers. An attending physician statement can reveal diagnosis, treatment, recovery, and prognosis, while further tests may clarify current mortality risk. A questionnaire alone would leave important evidence unresolved. Automatic standard issue would ignore the cardiac history, and a group underwriter does not handle an individual application merely because the applicant may work.
  24. The correct answer is C (Coverage continues while required premiums remain paid, despite later illness): Health evidence is used when the insurer decides whether and on what terms to issue the original policy. After issue, a later health decline does not give the insurer authority to cancel the contract, reduce a purchased benefit, or require new underwriting while the owner meets the contract obligations. A cancellation arising from nonpayment or another contractual lapse is a different issue. The other statements describe unilateral changes that standard individual whole life terms do not permit.
  25. The correct answer is A (Group guaranteed issue occurs within a defined non evidence maximum limit): Group contracts commonly permit eligible members to enroll for coverage up to a non evidence maximum during the initial eligibility period without health questions. This automatic acceptance feature explains the salary based benefit described. Individual underwriting would require personal evidence for each employee and is not what occurred. The employer does not guarantee personal health, and non evidence limits are a recognized group underwriting feature rather than an individual policy concept.
  26. The correct answer is B (It preserves treatment as life insurance under applicable tax qualification rules): The corridor maintains a minimum amount at risk between the death benefit and the accumulating fund. That life insurance element is required by the tax rules that determine whether the contract retains favorable tax treatment. The corridor is not merely an insurer margin device disconnected from qualification. It does not eliminate mortality deductions or guarantee a particular annual rate of account growth.
  27. The correct answer is C (Participating whole life coverage combining guarantees with possible dividends): Participating whole life combines guaranteed policy values with the possibility of dividends based on the insurer's actual experience. That description exactly matches scheduled guaranteed growth plus non guaranteed additions. Non participating whole life lacks the dividend feature. Term return of premium coverage does not provide whole life cash value growth, and indexed universal life ties crediting to an external index instead of participating dividends.
  28. The correct answer is D (A term rider sits within the base contract, while stand alone coverage is separate): A term rider is part of the permanent policy contract and is owned and administered with that base coverage. Stand alone term creates a separate contract with its own application, ownership, and administration. A rider can add death protection above the base benefit, so denying that function is incorrect. Relative premiums may vary, and a rider's term period can expire before the permanent base policy, so neither claim describes the defining structural difference.
  29. The correct answer is C (Rating reflects a recognized lifestyle habit associated with higher mortality risk): Smoking is a documented lifestyle factor linked to greater mortality risk, so insurers use it when assigning rate classes. With other evidence held comparable, that difference explains the separate premium treatment. Occupation is not the factor that changed here because the scenarios already control for comparable work. Family history is a separate underwriting consideration, and personal habits remain relevant after age is known.
  30. The correct answer is B (A benefit reduction schedule triggered by specified attained ages): The contract expressly lowers the amount of group life coverage by a stated percentage when the member reaches listed ages. That provision is a benefit reduction schedule, not an opportunity to increase coverage. Group term does not accumulate cash value. A scheduled reduction also does not convert the member's certificate into permanent individual insurance automatically.
  31. The correct answer is A (A flat extra adds fixed cost per thousand, while table rating adds percentage): A flat extra is a stated dollar charge for each thousand dollars of coverage and may be temporary or permanent depending on the risk. A table rating applies a percentage increase tied to the insurer's rating table, so the calculation method and resulting premium differ. The two approaches do not produce equivalent amounts in every case. Table ratings are used in individual underwriting, and a flat extra is not necessarily charged for the insured person's entire life.
  32. The correct answer is D (These activities can increase mortality risk and lead to ratings or exclusions): Scuba diving and private piloting can expose an applicant to a greater chance of accidental death even without a medical condition. Underwriters therefore use the details to decide whether the risk requires a higher premium or a specific activity exclusion. Such hazards are directly relevant to mortality assessment rather than unrelated lifestyle curiosity. The information is not gathered mainly for marketing and is not confined to determining employer group eligibility.
  33. The correct answer is D (Any unpaid loan balance and accrued interest reduce the beneficiary's death payment): The insurer offsets the policy debt, including accumulated interest, when it pays the death benefit. The beneficiary therefore receives the stated benefit less that outstanding balance. A policy loan has a direct effect when left unpaid rather than being economically neutral. Policy loans are normally available from the insurer against cash value, and borrowing does not automatically cancel future dividend eligibility.
  34. The correct answer is A (It provides objective medical evidence for assessing mortality risk and amount): A medical exam and blood profile give the insurer clinical measurements that can confirm or contradict self reported application answers. Those findings help determine whether a large requested face amount can be issued and on what rating basis. The process is not a test of the advisor's provincial licence. It does not primarily verify employment income or select an employer group plan, because those questions serve different financial and group underwriting functions.
  35. The correct answer is C (Joint last to die permanent coverage paying after the second death): A joint last to die policy insures two people and pays after both have died. That matches an estate obligation expected to arise at the second death and provides permanent coverage for the timing need. Joint first to die coverage would pay too early because it responds to the first death. Separate employer group certificates are employment linked and pay individually, while single life renewable term covers one person and lacks the requested permanent survivor structure.
  36. The correct answer is B (The classification and premium generally remain fixed after policy issue): The insurer sets the rating class from evidence obtained during underwriting, and that class generally stays with the issued contract. A later health decline or routine annual checkup does not reopen the underwriting decision or raise the existing premium. Rating does not reset at standard policy renewal dates or apply for only one year. Insurers also do not automatically reclassify a policy because a doctor reports a new condition after issue.
  37. The correct answer is B (Optional or voluntary supplemental coverage offered under group plans): Many group plans let eligible members elect extra coverage beyond the employer funded basic amount. The member usually pays that additional premium through payroll deduction, and evidence requirements depend on the plan's elective limits. Mandatory basic coverage supplies only the required base benefit and does not explain the top up. Conversion exists for coverage ending, not active employee enhancement, and waiver of premium protects premium payment during disability rather than adding voluntary insurance.
  38. The correct answer is A (Whole life coverage bundling costs, expenses, and values within one premium): Traditional whole life combines mortality costs, expenses, and cash value development inside a single level premium managed by the insurer. The policyowner does not direct separate investment accounts or see the components priced individually. Universal life is the unbundled alternative described in the question. Renewable term has no long term cash value, and employer group term provides temporary group protection without a personal bundled savings component.
  39. The correct answer is D (The withdrawal amount minus the adjusted cost basis allocated to that partial withdrawal): For a partial withdrawal, the adjusted cost basis allocated to the disposition is the full adjusted cost basis multiplied by the withdrawal proceeds divided by the cash surrender value immediately before the withdrawal, with the prescribed reduction for an outstanding policy loan. The taxable policy gain is the proceeds minus that allocated basis. Comparing the withdrawal with the entire contract basis would leave unused basis attached to the remaining policy and can materially misstate the tax. Term or permanent labelling, beneficiary revocability, and original applicant status do not determine the gain.
  40. The correct answer is C (The taxable policy gain realized from the reported withdrawal transaction): A disposition such as a withdrawal can produce a policy gain equal to proceeds above adjusted cost basis. The insurer reports that taxable gain on a T5 slip so the owner can include the amount in income for the relevant year. A future death benefit is not the transaction being reported by the slip. Scheduled premiums and remaining face amount are policy administration figures rather than taxable policy gain.
  41. The correct answer is B (The excess credits the corporation's capital dividend account balance): For corporate owned life insurance, the death benefit above adjusted cost basis generally increases the capital dividend account. The corporation can later use that balance to pay eligible tax free capital dividends to shareholders. The excess is not ordinary corporate income merely because the corporation received the policy proceeds. It does not reduce paid up capital, and tax law provides no thirty day mandatory distribution period.
  42. The correct answer is C (A disposition occurs, making any gain above adjusted cost basis taxable): Transferring ownership of a life policy is a disposition even when the transfer is a family gift. Because this policy insures Priya rather than her son, the special rollover for a gift of a policy on the child's life does not apply. The transferor generally recognizes a policy gain to the extent the applicable deemed proceeds exceed adjusted cost basis in the transfer year. Taxation is not deferred to a later surrender by the son or to the eventual death benefit payment.
  43. The correct answer is A (When the loan proceeds exceed the adjusted cost basis allocated to the disposed interest): A policy loan is a disposition of part of the policy interest. The taxable policy gain is the amount, if any, by which the loan proceeds exceed the adjusted cost basis allocated to that interest under the partial disposition rule. For a policy issued after 2016, calculate the allocated basis using the proceeds divided by the cash surrender value immediately before the loan after reducing that value by any existing policy loan. The intended use and repayment date do not eliminate a gain already realized.
  44. The correct answer is D (Prescribed group term coverage benefits, net of employee contributions, are taxable employment income): The employer funded group term benefit is taxable employment income for the covered employee. The amount is generally prescribed from the term coverage and related benefit rules, with employee paid amounts reducing the taxable benefit. The employee cannot deduct the value from other income. Group coverage is not automatically exempt, and no one million dollar threshold begins the reporting obligation.
  45. The correct answer is B (Annual accrued income inside the contract becomes taxable to the owner): A policy that becomes non exempt loses the usual tax shelter for its accumulating income, so the owner must include annual accrued income under the applicable rules. The policy itself can continue rather than requiring cancellation. A death benefit paid to a named beneficiary generally retains its separate tax treatment and does not become fully taxable merely because the policy failed the exempt test. Beneficiary designation rights are also unaffected by that testing failure.
  46. The correct answer is A (The annual net cost of pure insurance applied against the policy): Adjusted cost basis is reduced by the annual net cost of pure insurance charged for the mortality protection during the year. This deduction reflects the shifting balance between protection and accumulating policy value as the contract ages. It can reduce basis even without owner initiated withdrawals or changes to the death benefit. Administrative fee labelling, investment market swings, and face amount reductions are not the principal annual basis adjustment described here.
  47. The correct answer is C (The death benefit is received free from personal income tax reporting): A life insurance death benefit paid to a named beneficiary is generally received free of personal income tax. The widow therefore does not report the lump sum as ordinary income on her return. There is no rule requiring only proceeds above the original face amount to be included. She does not have to report the entire payment or spread an income inclusion over five following years.
  48. The correct answer is D (They are non deductible expenses when computing corporate taxable income): Premiums for life insurance owned by a corporation on a controlling shareholder are generally treated as capital related outlays rather than ordinary operating expenses. They therefore do not reduce the corporation's taxable income in the usual estate planning arrangement. A later resignation does not turn the past premium into a general business deduction. The tax rules also provide no fifty percent partial deduction formula for this type of life insurance premium.
  49. The correct answer is A (The gain is taxed fully as ordinary income during the surrender year): A surrender is a disposition, and proceeds above adjusted cost basis create a policy gain included in income for that year. This gain is not treated like a capital gain eligible for partial inclusion. The special tax treatment of a death benefit does not extend to a cash surrender gain during the owner's lifetime. The owner's age at surrender does not delay or remove the reporting obligation.
  50. The correct answer is B (Corporate ownership can add death benefit proceeds to the capital dividend account): When a corporation owns the policy, the death benefit above adjusted cost basis can increase its capital dividend account. That balance may support tax free capital dividends to shareholders after the insured's death. Ownership structure does not itself guarantee lower premiums or a higher cash surrender value because underwriting and contract design drive those results. Corporate ownership also does not remove exempt test requirements, so tax deferral inside the policy must still be monitored.
  51. The correct answer is C (They represent a partial return of premiums and are generally received tax-free.): policyholder dividends on participating coverage are generally treated as a return of part of the premiums rather than investment income. That treatment explains why receiving the annual dividend in cash normally does not create taxable income. The other choices confuse the dividend with annual interest, a capital gain, or deferred investment growth. Treating the dividend as fully taxable investment income would therefore misstate its usual return of premium treatment.
  52. The correct answer is D (A buy-sell agreement funded by life insurance on each partner's life.): the agreement creates the purchase obligation, and policies on both owners supply cash when one death occurs. This keeps the buyout from consuming working capital needed for manufacturing operations. Critical illness and disability expense products cover different risks, while personal coverage without a purchase agreement creates no enforced transfer mechanism. The alternative reflects a distinct error: funds illness costs instead of a death-triggered ownership purchase.
  53. The correct answer is D (A corporate share redemption or promissory note buy-sell arrangement.): corporate ownership and beneficiary status allow the company to use proceeds to redeem the deceased shareholder's shares, sometimes with a promissory note for the balance. A cross purchase plan places ownership among shareholders themselves, while split dollar funding shares costs between parties rather than describing this redemption structure. Key person coverage protects the business from an executive loss and does not itself transfer shares. Assigning ownership to the shareholders would fail to describe this corporate redemption structure.
  54. The correct answer is A (Corporate-owned key person insurance funded and payable to the business.): the corporation receives proceeds when the architect dies and can fund interim leadership, project recovery, and investor stabilization. A personal term policy protects his household rather than the company, and a buy sell agreement funds an ownership transfer instead of operational continuity. Lease related mortgage coverage responds to premises debt rather than the loss of a critical founder. Applying personal coverage to the company's continuity risk would leave the intended business need underfunded.
  55. The correct answer is C (It verifies the coverage amount against the executive's economic value.): financial underwriting examines revenue, duties, replacement costs, and disruption exposure to keep the requested face amount reasonable. This protects the insurer and the company from overinsuring one person. Medical evidence remains a separate underwriting input, and group pricing or contestability timing does not depend on this financial analysis. Using a financial review to promise a group discount would incorrectly substitute business analysis for group eligibility and pricing rules.
  56. The correct answer is B (It reduces later price disputes between the estate and surviving owners.): a pre agreed formula gives both sides a known valuation method before a death occurs. That clarity supports an orderly transfer during a stressful period and limits litigation over the company's value. The formula does not determine the policy premium or death benefit, and insurance funding may still be needed to provide purchase cash. Treating the valuation formula as a premium device would confuse the purchase agreement with the insurance contract.
  57. The correct answer is D (The corporation may retain, surrender, or reassign the owned contract.): ownership gives the corporation control after the insured executive leaves, subject to the policy terms and applicable tax and assignment rules. It may decide whether continued coverage still serves a business purpose, cash in the contract, or transfer it with the insurer's required steps. Neither automatic employee ownership nor premium refunds follow from resignation, and the contract does not become group coverage by itself. Assuming an automatic transfer or cancellation would disregard the corporation's ownership and the assignment process.
  58. The correct answer is C (The number of required policies multiplies rapidly with each added owner.): in a cross purchase plan, each owner normally needs a policy on every other owner, so ownership growth increases policy count, premiums, and administration sharply. Some plans use partnerships or other structures to manage that complexity, but the pairwise demand remains the central scaling problem. Death benefits do not become taxable merely through this structure, and the arrangement does not remove expense deductions or force surrender of personal coverage. Treating the plan's death benefits as taxable would confuse a valid tax rule with the structure's real scaling drawback.
  59. The correct answer is B (The risk that the founder's unexpected death prevents orderly loan repayment.): a lender may require coverage when repayment depends heavily on one leader's skills, relationships, or continued revenue contribution. Insurance proceeds can support debt service or repayment while the business adjusts to the loss. That protection does not control interest rate movements or fundraising success, and it does not insure declines in the founder's personal investment portfolio. Using mortality coverage to manage interest rate exposure would misidentify the risk the lender is protecting against.
  60. The correct answer is A (It supplements funds available for a lifetime buyout after retirement or disability.): accumulated cash value can be accessed, subject to policy terms and tax rules, to help finance a living buyout of a co-owner. It therefore supports succession planning beyond the death benefit alone. The cash value does not repay a commercial mortgage automatically, replace professional liability coverage, or become a registered pension plan when issued. The alternative reflects a distinct error: connects policy cash value with automatic mortgage repayment.
  61. The correct answer is D (It prevents life insurance from becoming a speculative wager on a person's life.): insurable interest confirms a genuine financial relationship or exposure that supports legitimate coverage. In a current buy sell arrangement, the corporation's economic stake in each partner can establish that interest at issue. The rule is not a claims guarantee, does not remove medical underwriting, and does not set mortality rate classification. Treating insurable interest as a guarantee of payment would confuse a validity requirement with the insurer's later claims assessment.
  62. The correct answer is B (Supplying liquidity for estate settlement and inheritance equalization.): proceeds can provide cash for estate obligations and allow non business heirs to receive comparable value without forcing a sale of the company. Insurance supports the estate plan but does not replace a will or the advice needed to coordinate ownership, beneficiary, and tax issues. It cannot promise future profits and does not erase probate fees because those depend on provincial law and how assets pass. Treating insurance as a substitute for the complete estate plan would overstate what the policy can accomplish.
  63. The correct answer is A (Confirming the requested face amount is proportionate to executive economic value.): audited results help an insurer judge the executive's contribution and the financial harm that could follow the person's death. This is financial underwriting aimed at avoiding excessive key person coverage. Driving history and substance use belong to personal risk assessment, while the contract, not company statements, establishes incontestability timing. The alternative reflects a distinct error: uses corporate financial records to assess driving history.
  64. The correct answer is C (How policy count and administration will scale as new owners join.): a cross purchase plan normally requires a policy between each pair of owners, while a corporate redemption structure usually needs one policy per insured shareholder. Expected growth therefore affects cost, record keeping, and operational complexity substantially. Equipment replacement, billing preferences, and mailing addresses do not determine the ownership and funding structure. Basing the structure on equipment or billing details would ignore the ownership and administration consequences at issue.
  65. The correct answer is A (An attending physician statement summarizing relevant medical history.): the treating doctor can provide clinical findings, diagnoses, treatment dates, test results, and prognosis for the disclosed cardiac and blood pressure concerns. That information helps the underwriter assess mortality risk accurately. Tax notices and employer letters are financial records, and a spouse's general observations do not supply the medical verification requested. The alternative reflects a distinct error: uses tax information to verify a cardiac diagnosis.
  66. The correct answer is C (Detecting undisclosed conditions or inconsistencies across prior applications.): the Medical Information Bureau uses coded records from member insurers to alert underwriters to possible omissions or conflicts in an applicant's disclosures. Those alerts may prompt further medical evidence and a more complete risk assessment. The database does not calculate the final premium, verify employment income, or establish insurable interest. The alternative reflects a distinct error: treats coded alert records as final premium calculation.
  67. The correct answer is D (Collecting objective health evidence supporting accurate mortality classification.): blood pressure, laboratory, and urine findings give underwriters measurable information that supplements the application answers. That evidence helps assign an appropriate mortality rate class and may reveal risks that a questionnaire alone would miss. Paramedical evidence does not apply only to renewable term, replace the questionnaire, or determine beneficiary eligibility. Limiting paramedical evidence to renewable term would misstate its wider role in mortality assessment.
  68. The correct answer is B (The preferred or preferred plus class reserved for lowest risk applicants.): insurers use preferred categories when favorable health, lifestyle, and family history indicate mortality below the standard range. Exact names and criteria vary by insurer, but these classes generally receive the most favorable nonsmoker pricing. Standard, substandard, and temporary classifications do not describe the stated lowest risk outcome. Using average mortality to classify a lowest risk applicant would ignore the favorable evidence supplied.
  69. The correct answer is C (A substandard or rated class reflecting materially higher mortality risk.): substandard, also called rated, classifications allow coverage despite mortality risk above the insurer's standard range. The insurer may assign an extra premium or a table rating based on the impairment and overall profile. Preferred classes reward unusually favorable risk, standard describes ordinary mortality, and juvenile classes concern younger ages rather than impairment. Placing a chronic impairment in the best health class would contradict the stated mortality finding.
  70. The correct answer is A (The chance that coverage creates a financial incentive for wrongdoing.): moral hazard focuses on conduct risk created by insurance, particularly when excessive coverage or an inappropriate beneficiary may encourage harmful choices. Financial underwriting and insurable interest checks help limit that concern. Investment return, disability, and inflation are separate financial or morbidity risks rather than moral hazard. Confusing investment performance risk with conduct risk would misstate the underwriting concern.
  71. The correct answer is B (Physical, occupational, and avocational hazards affecting mortality risk.): underwriting considers health, work duties, and leisure activities together when they can affect mortality. Commercial flying and recreational diving therefore enter the assessment through occupational and avocational hazard analysis. Income source does not make the diving irrelevant, and financial underwriting does not replace the review of these physical risks. The alternative reflects a distinct error: ignores nonoccupational diving exposure.
  72. The correct answer is D (The incontestability clause, including whether its contestability period expired.): the clause limits the time in which an insurer may challenge coverage based on material misrepresentation in the application. The death timing, policy terms, and applicable provincial insurance law must all be checked to determine whether contestation remains available. Suicide, premium grace, and free look provisions address different rights and events. The alternative reflects a distinct error: uses the suicide exclusion to analyze cancer concealment.
  73. The correct answer is A (A free look period allowing cancellation shortly after policy delivery.): free look rights give a buyer a short review window and allow cancellation for a refund under the policy and provincial requirements. They exist to protect consumers after the contract is delivered. Grace protects coverage after late payment, incontestability limits policy challenges, and reinstatement revives lapsed coverage. The alternative reflects a distinct error: replaces the review refund right with premium grace.
  74. The correct answer is D (Whether the requested coverage amount is reasonably justified and not excessive.): income, net worth, and existing coverage help the insurer evaluate the proposed benefit against the applicant's financial circumstances. This review limits overinsurance and concerns associated with excessive coverage. Health habits are medical underwriting factors, beneficiary interest is a legal relationship question, and Canadian coverage amounts are not governed by the fixed statutory ceiling described. The alternative reflects a distinct error: uses financial records to classify personal health habits.
  75. The correct answer is C (A material misrepresentation allowing the insurer to contest the claim.): an omission that could reasonably influence underwriting may be treated as material, particularly during the contestability period. The insurer can then assess remedies under the policy and applicable provincial law, which may include rescission or denial depending on the facts. The missing cardiac information is not necessarily immaterial, routine, or a beneficiary correction issue. The alternative reflects a distinct error: assumes a cardiac diagnosis omission lacks underwriting importance.
  76. The correct answer is B (Risk classification reflects the applicant's overall mortality profile.): diabetes underwriting considers control, complications, duration, age, and other health factors together. Insurers use that full picture to place the applicant in an available rate class rather than relying on one isolated detail. Current readings and diagnosis duration matter, but neither determines the outcome alone, and chronic conditions do not automatically preclude coverage. The alternative reflects a distinct error: reduces mortality assessment to one blood sugar result.
  77. The correct answer is A (The exempt test comparing the policy with a benchmark test policy.): the annual exempt test checks accumulated policy values against a prescribed benchmark to determine whether tax-exempt treatment continues. A policy that fails can become non-exempt and face annual accrual taxation. Participation, capital adequacy, and insurable interest describe other insurance concepts and do not perform this federal tax comparison. The alternative reflects a distinct error: mistakes dividend participation rules for the tax exemption test.
  78. The correct answer is C (The cumulative net cost of pure insurance charged against the contract.): net cost of pure insurance reflects the mortality cost of protection and generally reduces adjusted cost basis each year under the prescribed tax calculation. This can cause the basis to decline as a permanent policy ages. Advisor commissions, loan interest, and illustrated dividend scales are not the standard annual ACB deduction described. The alternative reflects a distinct error: treats advisor compensation as an annual ACB reduction.
  79. The correct answer is B (When the borrowed amount exceeds the adjusted cost basis allocated to the loan.): Taking a policy loan is a disposition of part of the policy interest. Any taxable policy gain arises when the loan proceeds exceed the adjusted cost basis allocated to that part under the proportional rule. The use of borrowed funds and dividend timing do not determine the policy gain, and equaling cash surrender value is not the statutory test. The correct comparison therefore uses the allocated adjusted cost basis rather than the policy's total adjusted cost basis.
  80. The correct answer is D (The withdrawal amount minus the adjusted cost basis allocated to it.): a partial policy withdrawal creates a gain when the amount received exceeds the portion of adjusted cost basis allocated to that withdrawal. The allocation prevents the owner from using the same basis repeatedly. Full cash value, premium history, and net cost of pure insurance are not the direct subtraction formula for this partial disposition. The alternative reflects a distinct error: ignores the adjusted cost basis allocated to the withdrawal.
  81. The correct answer is D (The death benefit is generally received free of income tax.): a named individual beneficiary usually receives individual life insurance proceeds without income tax under the basic death benefit rule. The payment generally passes outside the estate as well when the designation is valid. It is not ordinary income, is not moved onto the estate return merely because of the death, and does not use an adjusted cost basis formula in the usual named-beneficiary payment. The alternative reflects a distinct error: treats the ordinary death benefit as personal income.
  82. The correct answer is A (Cash surrender value received minus the policy's adjusted cost basis.): a full surrender produces a policy gain when the cash surrender value exceeds the adjusted cost basis at that time. The basis represents the remaining tax cost of the contract, so it is subtracted rather than added. Death benefit comparisons and a separate premium minus mortality cost calculation do not state the full surrender gain formula. The alternative reflects a distinct error: builds a premium history formula instead of using final basis.
  83. The correct answer is B (The assignment itself creates no disposition or immediate taxable event.): collateral assignment gives the lender limited security rights while the policy owner generally retains ownership of the contract. Because the assignment is not an outright transfer of beneficial ownership, it does not ordinarily trigger a disposition or make the loan itself taxable income. Value-based disposition, automatic loss of exemption, and immediate income treatment describe different events. The alternative reflects a distinct error: treats collateral security as an immediate value disposition.
  84. The correct answer is C (The mortality cost of protection calculated under a prescribed formula.): net cost of pure insurance estimates the annual cost of the death protection element using tax rules and actuarial values. It is deducted in calculating adjusted cost basis and is not the policy's total expense or commission. Issue commissions, loan administration fees, and illustrated dividends serve different functions. The alternative reflects a distinct error: mistakes initial sales compensation for mortality protection cost.
  85. The correct answer is A (Internal investment growth avoids annual accrual taxation while it remains exempt.): An exempt policy can accumulate investment income inside the contract without the annual reporting that normally applies to a non-registered account. Tax may still arise when value is withdrawn or the policy is disposed of above the remaining basis. Withdrawals are not universally tax free, premiums are not generally employment deductions, and probate outcomes depend on beneficiary and estate arrangements. A client considering a cash-value withdrawal should compare the taxable policy gain with the remaining adjusted cost basis.
  86. The correct answer is B (Report the policy's accrued income growth annually on the tax return.): a non-exempt policy is generally taxed on an accrual basis, so the owner includes the year's internal investment income in taxable income. This resembles the annual treatment of a non-registered investment rather than the deferral available inside an exempt policy. No cash surrender, insurer penalty, or forced conversion to term coverage is required by non-exempt status alone. The alternative reflects a distinct error: requires partial surrender as the compliance response.
  87. The correct answer is C (Cumulative net cost of pure insurance reduces the adjusted cost basis annually.): as protection charges accumulate, the prescribed net cost of pure insurance can steadily reduce the policy's tax cost until the basis reaches zero. This is a routine feature of the annual adjusted cost basis calculation. Premiums can still affect basis, there is no age sixty-five step-down, and provincial authorities do not reset basis on a five year schedule. The alternative reflects a distinct error: stops future premiums from ever affecting adjusted cost basis.
  88. The correct answer is D (A deemed disposition occurs at the greater of cash value and adjusted cost basis.): transferring ownership to a non-spouse can trigger a disposition using this prescribed value, producing a taxable policy gain for the transferor in some cases. The daughter's new basis must then be considered under the rules for the transfer. Life insurance ownership is not universally exempt, tax is not necessarily postponed until death, and the original basis can change. The alternative reflects a distinct error: treats every ownership transfer as a tax-exempt event.
  89. The correct answer is A (It is taxable to the policyholder as interest income when credited.): interest earned on dividends left on deposit is generally reported annually because it is investment income credited during the year. The underlying dividend may be a tax-free return of premium, but the later interest is taxed differently. It is not deferred until surrender, added to basis, or treated like a tax-free death benefit. The alternative reflects a distinct error: defers annual interest income until the policy surrender.
  90. The correct answer is C (Naming the children directly so proceeds bypass the probate estate.): a valid named beneficiary designation generally allows the insurer to pay outside the estate and avoid estate probate administration for those proceeds. The advisor should still confirm capacity, revocation rules, guardianship for minors, and provincial requirements. Naming the estate or leaving the field blank can produce estate administration, while collateral security does not create the intended beneficiary route. The alternative reflects a distinct error: routes proceeds through the estate and into probate.
  91. The correct answer is B (The borrowed funds are used to earn business or investment income.): Interest may be deductible when borrowed money is used for an eligible income-earning purpose, subject to the ordinary interest deductibility rules. Purchasing business equipment can satisfy that purpose if the deduction criteria are met. The insurance connection alone does not qualify the interest, and the borrower's age is irrelevant. A client should document the direct business or investment use of the loan proceeds.
  92. The correct answer is D (The policy becomes non-exempt and accrued growth becomes annually taxable.): failing the in-force exempt test changes the contract's tax status and generally requires annual inclusion of its investment income. The underlying death benefit and ownership rights do not disappear because the accumulation test fails. Insurers are not required to cancel and refund the policy, beneficiaries are not made taxable by that event alone, and beneficiary designation rights continue. The alternative reflects a distinct error: requires insurer cancellation and complete premium refund.
  93. The correct answer is D (Only the ACB attributable to the withdrawn amount offsets the gain.): proportionate allocation matches tax basis with the portion of the policy value being removed. This measures the gain fairly while leaving basis for the value that remains in the contract. Using all basis at once would misstate future gains, and partial withdrawal neither guarantees tax freedom nor changes the transaction into a policy loan. The alternative reflects a distinct error: applies all available basis to one partial withdrawal.
  94. The correct answer is B (The adjusted cost basis is treated as zero rather than negative.): the tax calculation does not allow adjusted cost basis to fall below zero, even when cumulative protection costs exceed premium additions. A zero basis means future withdrawals or surrender can produce a larger gain because no cost remains to offset proceeds. The insurer does not pay a refund for the excess charge, and the contract does not convert automatically to reduced paid-up coverage. The alternative reflects a distinct error: allows adjusted cost basis to become a negative tax value.
  95. The correct answer is A (The proceeds form part of the estate and may attract probate fees.): estate beneficiary designation causes the insurer to pay the estate, where the money becomes subject to estate administration and applicable creditor claims. Probate treatment and fees vary by province, so the advisor must apply local law. The designation does not create universal tax exemption, require direct first payment to creditors, or impose an automatic equal division among heirs. The alternative reflects a distinct error: creates universal exemption from all provincial taxes.
  96. The correct answer is C (Exempt policy growth avoids annual taxation, so no T5 slip is issued.): While a policy remains exempt, internal investment income is not included in the owner's annual taxable income, so there is normally no annual T5 amount to report. A non-registered account follows different annual reporting rules. Insurers still track contract values for tax purposes, age seventy-one does not create this T5 rule, and no blanket reporting waiver covers every insurance product. The policy's contract values remain relevant if cash value is withdrawn or the policy is disposed of.
  97. The correct answer is C (It funds the surviving partner's purchase of the deceased partner's share.): the death benefit supplies cash for the purchase required by the buy-sell agreement without drawing on manufacturing working capital. This supports a timely transfer of ownership to the survivor. Disability income, family salary continuation, and general liability premiums address other needs and do not fund this death-triggered share purchase. The alternative reflects a distinct error: uses buy-sell proceeds for personal disability replacement.
  98. The correct answer is B (Key person coverage protecting the business from a critical employee's death.): corporate ownership and beneficiary status let the company receive proceeds for disruption caused by losing an employee whose work is central to revenue and delivery. This is key person risk, not an ownership transfer. Buy-sell funding addresses shareholders, split-dollar plans share costs and benefits, and creditor coverage responds to a lender's exposure. The alternative reflects a distinct error: treats corporate executive coverage as shareholder buy-sell funding.
  99. The correct answer is A (The criss-cross method of funding a buy-sell agreement.): criss-cross ownership means each partner owns a policy on each other partner, so death proceeds flow to surviving owners for the buyout. Corporate redemption instead uses company-owned policies and corporate funds, while a promissory note relies on payments to complete the purchase. Collateral assignment secures a lender and does not describe this mutual ownership pattern. The alternative reflects a distinct error: uses corporate-owned redemption for pairwise shareholder policies.
  100. The correct answer is D (The capital dividend account, which permits a tax-free capital dividend.): a private corporation can add the qualifying tax-free portion of life insurance proceeds to its capital dividend account and use the balance for an eligible capital dividend. This tax account is distinct from retained earnings, shareholder loans, and refundable dividend tax on hand. The amounts and eligibility still require correct corporate tax calculations and formal dividend procedures. The alternative reflects a distinct error: uses the general profit account instead of the capital dividend account.
  101. The correct answer is C (The death benefit received less the policy's adjusted cost basis at death): The capital dividend account credit from a corporate-owned life policy equals the death benefit received minus the policy's adjusted cost basis at death. The gross face amount and cumulative premiums are different measures that can overstate or understate that credit. Annual corporate net income belongs to ordinary tax reporting and does not form part of this policy calculation. The alternative reflects a distinct error: uses gross face value and ignores the adjusted cost basis deduction.
  102. The correct answer is B (A formal independent business valuation for each ownership interest): A current business valuation gives the shareholders and insurer a defensible dollar value for each ownership interest. Personal tax returns show individual income, while liability coverage responds to third-party claims and does not measure business equity. The founding year describes history rather than the present value of the shares. The alternative reflects a distinct error: measures shareholder personal earnings instead of corporate ownership value.
  103. The correct answer is D (Generally not deductible when the corporation owns coverage and receives the benefit): Key person life insurance premiums are generally non-deductible when the corporation owns the policy and receives the death benefit. The policy payout is treated as a capital receipt rather than income replacement. A director title and the size of the death benefit do not create a premium deduction under these ownership terms. The alternative reflects a distinct error: assumes corporate ownership alone turns insurance premiums into ordinary expenses.
  104. The correct answer is A (The manufacturer demonstrates insurable interest in the supplier owner's life): Insurable interest means the manufacturer would suffer a genuine financial loss if the supplier's owner died. Dependence on a specialized supplier can support that business relationship. Employment status, majority share ownership, and a shared accountant are not required to prove the applicant's legal interest in the insured life. The alternative reflects a distinct error: requires employment rather than a genuine financial loss from death.
  105. The correct answer is C (The surviving partner's future cash flow may prove insufficient): A promissory note funded from future business profits depends on the surviving partner's ability to generate enough cash after the death. An early death can leave the business without adequate accumulated funds. The arrangement does not create immediate full payment, remove the written agreement, or create policy proceeds for the capital dividend account. The funding plan should be stress tested against weak profits and early death.
  106. The correct answer is A (A shareholder suffers permanent disability preventing work in the business): Shareholders' agreements commonly list permanent disability that prevents active work as a buyout trigger. The event affects the owner's ability to contribute and can create the exit contemplated by the agreement. A vacation, promotion, or spouse's job change does not establish that permanent disability. The alternative reflects a distinct error: treats brief absence from work as permanent inability to contribute.
  107. The correct answer is B (A split-dollar arrangement sharing premiums and benefits between employer and employee): A split-dollar arrangement divides premium costs and contract benefits under an agreement between an employer and employee. The other choices describe different transactions involving business partners, corporate tax elections, or lender security. None of them matches the employee policy with shared premium and benefit rights. The alternative reflects a distinct error: describes partner purchase rights rather than employer employee benefit sharing.
  108. The correct answer is D (To repay the bank from proceeds if the owner dies before repayment): A collateral assignment gives the lender a claim on policy proceeds up to the outstanding loan balance if the insured dies before repayment. This protects the bank without changing the policy into a retirement plan. The assignment does not raise the policy premium or suspend loan payments during the owner's lifetime. The alternative reflects a distinct error: associates loan security with an increase in insurance premium rates.
  109. The correct answer is B (Disability buyout coverage funding a purchase after qualifying disability): Disability buyout insurance provides funds for the ownership purchase when a partner meets the contract's disability definition. It addresses an exit event that life insurance does not cover. A larger death benefit and another life arrangement still depend on death, while commercial liability coverage responds to third-party business claims. The alternative reflects a distinct error: keeps the funding tied to death despite the disability exit need.
  110. The correct answer is D (A taxable shareholder benefit may reflect the policy's fair market value): A corporation that transfers a life policy to a shareholder can confer a taxable benefit on that shareholder. The policy's fair market value is a central measure because the shareholder receives an asset from the corporation. The transaction is not automatically tax-free, does not force a surrender, and ends the corporation's ownership rights. The alternative reflects a distinct error: treats a transfer of corporate value as tax neutral without analysis.
  111. The correct answer is A (The agreed valuation method using earnings multiples or business assets): The valuation method translates business performance and assets into a dollar amount for each ownership interest. That figure directly sets the insurance amount needed to fund the buyout. Retirement timing, branding choices, and short-term hiring totals do not replace an agreed valuation method. The alternative reflects a distinct error: uses retirement timing as a proxy instead of a defined valuation.
  112. The correct answer is C (The financial loss the business expects after the founder's premature death): Key person coverage is sized from the loss the business would face, including lost earnings, contract disruption, and replacement costs. The founder's personal policy does not measure the company's exposure. A spouse's share ownership and vacation preferences do not establish the operational loss from the founder's death. The alternative reflects a distinct error: uses coverage bought for personal protection to size a business loss.
  113. The correct answer is A (The fund may hold too little cash for an early unexpected owner death): A sinking fund accumulates gradually, so an early death can occur before retained cash reaches the agreed purchase price. Life insurance can deliver the face amount when the insured dies. The method does not force dissolution, create an annual capital gain by itself, or prevent the arrangement from being documented. The agreement should compare the accumulated fund with the current buyout obligation.
  114. The correct answer is B (File the prescribed election with the Canada Revenue Agency): A corporation must file the prescribed capital dividend election and supporting records when paying the eligible amount. The credit is based on qualifying additions to the capital dividend account rather than ordinary income. There is no five-year waiting rule or court approval step in the normal election process. The alternative reflects a distinct error: adds an unsupported five year period before a permitted distribution.
  115. The correct answer is C (Preferred or standard plus class reflecting lower mortality risk): Preferred and standard plus classifications recognize favorable health evidence and lower expected mortality. The applicant's stable weight, normal results, tobacco-free status, and limited family history support that placement. Substandard and graded classes address elevated risk, while a declined decision would reject coverage rather than recognize favorable findings. The alternative reflects a distinct error: places favorable evidence in a class designed for elevated mortality.
  116. The correct answer is D (Non-medical underwriting limits permitting policy issue without examination): Non-medical limits set age-based coverage amounts below which evidence such as a paramedical exam or laboratory tests may not be required. The question's simplified process fits that underwriting rule. Tax qualification, incontestability timing, and the corporate capital dividend account serve different purposes. The alternative reflects a distinct error: connects an examination waiver with policy tax qualification testing.
  117. The correct answer is D (The Medical Information Bureau shared coded underwriting database): The Medical Information Bureau maintains coded information that member life insurers use to detect inconsistent or omitted disclosures across applications. It supports underwriting review and does not replace the insurer's own decision. The tax agency, vital statistics office, and a private capital dividend ledger do not perform this shared underwriting function. The alternative reflects a distinct error: assigns shared medical underwriting data to the federal tax agency.
  118. The correct answer is B (The duty to disclose material facts truthfully and completely): Applicants must provide complete and truthful answers about facts that could affect the insurer's underwriting decision. A diagnosed health condition known to the applicant is material when the application asks about it. Beneficiary appointment, premium mode selection, and examination timing are separate procedural matters. The alternative reflects a distinct error: confuses beneficiary decision making with the applicant disclosure obligation.
  119. The correct answer is A (Investigate and potentially deny the claim within the contestability period): During the contestability period, an insurer may investigate whether a material misrepresentation affected the underwriting decision. If the legal test is met, the claim may be denied under the contract and applicable law. Double payment, permanent rejection of the beneficiary, and automatic transfer of the insurer's decision are not contestability remedies. The alternative reflects a distinct error: invents an enhanced benefit payment rather than a contractual claim review.
  120. The correct answer is C (A flat extra premium charge tied to a temporary hazard): A flat extra adds a set dollar charge per thousand dollars of coverage for a defined period to reflect a specific hazard. Hazardous recreational activities can justify this temporary pricing adjustment. A graded benefit changes claim payments, a tax account does not rate mortality, and non-medical limits govern evidence requirements. The alternative reflects a distinct error: describes a payout schedule rather than a temporary mortality charge.
  121. The correct answer is C (An exclusion rider removing coverage for a specified medical condition): An exclusion rider narrows the contract so claims arising from a named condition are excluded while other coverage remains in force. It can allow an applicant with elevated medical risk to receive a modified policy instead of a decline. Capital dividend rules, non-medical limits, and business funding clauses do not perform that function. The alternative reflects a distinct error: confuses a corporate account restriction with a medical policy rider.
  122. The correct answer is D (An attending physician statement summarizing treating doctor records): An attending physician statement gathers detailed clinical history, test results, and the treating doctor's current opinion about a disclosed condition. That evidence supports a medically informed underwriting decision. The other documents concern corporate tax records, a business purchase agreement, or contract values rather than the applicant's medical history. The alternative reflects a distinct error: provides tax accounting confirmation instead of treating doctor evidence.
  123. The correct answer is B (A paramedical examination conducted by a qualified insurance examiner): A paramedical examination collects measurements, vital signs, and specimens for underwriting at a coverage level that requires medical evidence. The appointment does not produce a treating doctor's narrative or an agent's field notes. It also has no connection to a corporate tax account audit. The alternative reflects a distinct error: confuses a doctor records request with an in-person health examination.
  124. The correct answer is A (Field underwriting review performed by the agent at the point of sale): Field underwriting is the agent's front-end assessment of the applicant's stated health, occupation, and financial circumstances while completing the application. The observations help the insurer identify missing or inconsistent information. Reinsurance, corporate account reconciliation, and a post-issue claims review occur at different stages for different purposes. The alternative reflects a distinct error: describes insurer risk sharing rather than the agent's application meeting.
  125. The correct answer is A (A reinsurance arrangement sharing excess risk with another insurer): Reinsurance allows the original insurer to cede exposure above its retention limit and share related premium with another insurer. It directly manages concentration risk on larger policies. Field underwriting and criss-cross buy-sell planning serve different insurance roles, while capital dividend accounts are corporate tax records rather than a risk-sharing mechanism. The alternative reflects a distinct error: confuses front-end application review with intercompany risk transfer.
  126. The correct answer is D (A moral hazard concerning motive and financial justification): Moral hazard concerns whether an applicant's character or motive creates an unacceptable risk, including a coverage amount that is not financially justified. The mismatch between the requested benefit and the applicant's disclosed resources raises that question. Medical history, job duties, and country of residence describe physical, occupational, and residency hazards. The alternative reflects a distinct error: treats the applicant's medical history as evidence about financial motive.
  127. The correct answer is C (An added premium or temporary exclusion for the special risk): Hazardous work and travel to unstable regions can lead insurers to charge an additional premium or exclude the specific exposure. These terms reflect the higher uncertainty surrounding the applicant's occupation and travel pattern. They do not lead to preferred pricing, change beneficiary rights, or move the file into simplified issue automatically. The alternative reflects a distinct error: assumes risky work and travel qualify for the best published pricing.
  128. The correct answer is B (Postpone the decision until the required medical results arrive): Pending pathology and follow-up information can materially change the risk assessment, so the responsible action is to delay final classification until those results are available. Issuing immediately or assigning preferred status would decide the case without essential evidence. The Medical Information Bureau shares coded information and does not approve insurance applications. The alternative reflects a distinct error: finalizes standard pricing without resolving the pending medical question.
  129. The correct answer is D (Whether replacement restarts contestability and requires current insurability): A new contract generally requires the client to qualify again under current health evidence and begins a new contestability period. Because her health has changed, those conditions create a meaningful risk that the lower quote does not resolve. Premium refunds, automatic corporate tax treatment, and removal of beneficiary requirements are not standard replacement outcomes. The alternative reflects a distinct error: assumes replacement creates a refund of premiums paid to the old insurer.
  130. The correct answer is B (Clarify the discrepancy and confirm the accurate answer): The agent should discuss the inconsistent information with the client, identify the correct fact, and ensure the application reflects it before submission. That protects the integrity of underwriting and the client's disclosure obligations. Ignoring the conflict, editing it without consent, or leaving a material answer blank does not resolve the uncertainty. The alternative reflects a distinct error: submits knowingly inconsistent information without resolving the conflict.
  131. The correct answer is A (The insurer generally cannot deny payment from that inaccuracy alone): Once the standard contestability period has passed, a non-fraudulent error generally no longer gives the insurer a basis to deny the claim. The promised benefit is then protected from that application inaccuracy. There is no general ten-year denial right, automatic benefit reduction, or special minor-beneficiary exception described here. The alternative reflects a distinct error: extends claim contest rights far beyond the stated contract period.
  132. The correct answer is C (Informed written consent obtained before the testing begins): HIV testing is treated as sensitive personal health information, and provincial insurance rules require the applicant's written consent before collection. That specific authorization must come directly from the applicant and identify the test being authorized. A broad liability release, a different application form, or bureau approval does not replace that authorization. The alternative reflects a distinct error: substitutes a broad risk release for specific informed testing consent.
  133. The correct answer is A (A table rating increasing premium according to impairment severity): A table rating assigns a numbered risk class above standard and applies a corresponding premium increase for a chronic impairment. The severity of the condition determines the rating level. Non-medical limits concern evidence requirements, capital dividend accounts are tax records, and agents do not set final insurer mortality rates. The alternative reflects a distinct error: confuses a medical evidence threshold with an impairment premium rating.
  134. The correct answer is D (The income replacement approach estimating future lost earnings): Income replacement estimates the pay dependents would lose over the insured's remaining working period and sizes protection around that loss. Taxes and personal consumption refine the amount of earnings available to the family. Capital needs analysis uses a broader expense framework, while the exempt test and buy-sell valuation address tax classification and business ownership. The alternative reflects a distinct error: uses a general capital depletion strategy rather than projected lost pay.
  135. The correct answer is C (The human life value method projecting future earnings): Human life value estimates the insured person's economic contribution by projecting future earnings, deducting taxes and personal use, and discounting the net amount to present value. Capital retention focuses on preserving principal for ongoing income, while needs analysis compares specific obligations with available resources. A business valuation measures enterprise ownership rather than personal earning capacity. The alternative reflects a distinct error: measures income produced by preserved capital rather than future pay.
  136. The correct answer is B (The outstanding mortgage balance and funeral and final expenses): Immediate lump-sum needs include obligations that arise around the death and require funds quickly, such as the mortgage balance and funeral costs. Monthly groceries and entertainment belong with continuing household expenses. Advisor commissions and insurer reserves are business accounting items, not needs of the client's family. The alternative reflects a distinct error: places recurring household spending in the immediate lump sum category.
  137. The correct answer is D (Capital retention and capital depletion income funding approaches): Capital retention preserves principal and uses investment income for family support, while capital depletion uses both income and principal until the fund is exhausted. These terms describe the two funding patterns in the question. Preferred and substandard classes relate to mortality risk, exempt status concerns tax treatment, and buyout methods fund business purchases. A funding recommendation should use the term that matches the client's intended pattern of withdrawals.
  138. The correct answer is B (To gather facts needed for a suitable insurance recommendation): The interview collects the client's financial position, dependents, coverage, health considerations, and objectives. Those facts allow the agent to recommend a product and amount that fit the client's circumstances. Capital dividend eligibility, exempt test calculations, and net cost of pure insurance are technical policy or tax exercises rather than the purpose of initial fact finding. The alternative reflects a distinct error: mistakes personal fact finding for corporate account eligibility testing.
  139. The correct answer is A (The suitability obligation commonly described as know your client): The agent is using the client's circumstances to establish that the recommended policy and amount are suitable. Clear file notes document the link between the facts and the advice.
