Tax elections, paramedical examinations, and bureau registration are separate administrative or underwriting steps rather than the core suitability duty. The alternative reflects a distinct error: applies a corporate tax filing duty to a personal suitability decision.
  140. The correct answer is C (Conduct a periodic policy review reassessing the client's current needs): A periodic review lets the advisor compare the existing policy with the client's current dependents, debt, and objectives. Marriage, a child, and a larger mortgage are meaningful changes that can alter the needed coverage. Passive waiting, automatic replacement, and transferring all service responsibility do not meet the ongoing review purpose. The alternative reflects a distinct error: leaves major changes without a proactive coverage reassessment.
  141. The correct answer is C (Recommend individual coverage closing the remaining protection gap): Existing group coverage provides two hundred thousand dollars toward a six hundred thousand dollar need, leaving a four hundred thousand dollar gap. Individual coverage can fill the unprotected amount in a way the client can afford. Cancelling group coverage would increase the gap, a bureau does not receive shortfall reports, and a buy-sell valuation does not apply to this family need. The alternative reflects a distinct error: removes useful existing protection and expands the remaining shortfall.
  142. The correct answer is D (An education funding need within the capital needs analysis): The projected post-secondary costs represent a specific future obligation of the family, so they appear as an education funding need in the capital analysis. The line ensures money would be available if the parent died before enrollment. Business continuation, final expenses, and a corporate capital dividend credit address different planning purposes. The alternative reflects a distinct error: applies a business ownership transfer need to a child education cost.
  143. The correct answer is B (A debt elimination need clearing the outstanding mortgage balance): Paying off the home loan removes a fixed debt and allows the surviving family to remain in the home. The capital needs analysis therefore treats the mortgage balance as a debt elimination need. Key person insurance protects a business, a capital dividend account is a corporate tax measure, and non-medical limits concern underwriting evidence. The alternative reflects a distinct error: connects household mortgage protection to an employer business loss.
  144. The correct answer is A (The client adding a dependent and increasing the mortgage balance): A new child adds a long-term financial responsibility, while a larger mortgage increases the debt that could burden the family after death. Together, those changes can materially alter the required coverage. Routine licence renewal, payment timing, and an insurer address change do not ordinarily change insurance needs. The alternative reflects a distinct error: treats a routine licence renewal as a change in financial obligations.
  145. The correct answer is A (It records the suitability reasoning available for later review): The notes preserve how the agent connected the client's circumstances and objectives to the recommendation. A supervisor or regulator can then assess whether the advice was suitable at the time. Documentation does not replace future fact finding, determine tax qualification, or remove the client's application signature. The alternative reflects a distinct error: treats a past suitability record as a permanent replacement for discovery.
  146. The correct answer is C (Needs analysis targets specific expenses while human life value targets income): Needs analysis builds coverage from identified debts, expenses, and obligations, while human life value estimates the present worth of future earnings. The methods answer different financial questions and can therefore produce different amounts. Neither is restricted to corporate-owned policies or one insurance product type. The alternative reflects a distinct error: assumes different measurement bases must produce one common result.
  147. The correct answer is D (Investment risk tolerance remains separate from the insurance need calculation): Comfort with market volatility relates to an investment portfolio, while a needs or income analysis measures the financial loss that insurance must replace. A client's investment preferences do not change those obligations by themselves. Coverage should not be cancelled before analysis, and risk tolerance does not determine premium mode. The alternative reflects a distinct error: treats portfolio comfort as though it erased family financial obligations.

  218. The correct answer is A (They prevent a proposed individual benefit from overinsuring total disability income replacement.): Existing employer and government income is part of the client's total claim resources, so those amounts must be counted with the proposed individual benefit. This comparison helps keep combined monthly income within a reasonable replacement level and supports return-to-work incentives. Dental benchmarks, critical illness survival periods, and renewability categories are unrelated to that replacement calculation. The advisor should verify benefit definitions and exclusions because a stated group amount may not equal the cash available after tax.
  219. The correct answer is B (Ongoing business overhead expenses that continue while the consultant cannot perform work.): Rent, insurance, wages, and similar fixed costs can keep draining the practice even when consulting income stops. Business overhead expense coverage is designed to address that distinct exposure alongside personal disability income protection. A spouse's life insurance, a dental guide, and equipment specifications do not identify the recurring operating costs at issue. The advisor should compare the practice's fixed expenses and the available overhead contract, including its definitions, limits, and waiting period.
  220. The correct answer is D (The benefit should reflect the client's debts, treatment costs, and desired recovery cushion.): Critical illness coverage is often sized around the cash a client would want after diagnosis, including debt relief, non-insured treatment costs, travel, and time away from work. Those needs vary with finances and responsibilities, so no single standard amount fits everyone. An exact salary multiple, one national amount, and a regulator-set benefit each ignore personal circumstances. The advisor should document a practical target that the client understands and can afford.
  221. The correct answer is A (It may reduce the need for a large individual benefit after other gaps are reviewed.): A group plan that already replaces about two thirds of income can materially reduce the individual benefit needed, although portability, definitions, offsets, taxes, and maximums still require review. The recommendation should be based on the combined claim outcome rather than a sales target for either contract. Group coverage is relevant to the analysis, existing coverage does not make every individual contract unavailable, and critical illness protects a different financial need. Supplemental disability coverage may still be useful when the group plan has meaningful limitations.
  222. The correct answer is D (The advisor should compare the benefit after estimated tax with the household income it must replace.): An employer-paid premium that was not taxed to the employee generally leads to taxable disability income, so the stated monthly benefit is not the amount available for spending. A needs analysis should estimate the after-tax claim amount and compare that net figure with essential expenses. Tax materially changes purchasing power, valuable group coverage should not be discarded for tax reasons alone, and taxation can also affect individually owned coverage when premiums were deducted. The comparison should use current tax information with an appropriate professional caveat.
  223. The correct answer is B (Disability protection often addresses a probable loss of earned income that may continue for years.): A working client still needs income even without dependents, and a lengthy disability can affect housing, debt payments, and basic living costs. Disability coverage therefore deserves consideration when comparing the probability and severity of income loss with the cash needs created by a critical illness. Age alone does not remove the exposure, critical illness is not automatically the first product for every buyer, and dependence is not required before protection becomes relevant. The final priority should reflect the client's stated goals, affordability, and existing workplace benefits.
  224. The correct answer is C (Adjusting the elimination period, benefit period, or disability definition can lower the premium while preserving protection.): A longer waiting period, shorter payment duration, or less comprehensive disability definition may reduce cost while keeping core income protection. These tradeoffs should be explained so the client understands the coverage given up in exchange for affordability. Critical illness coverage does not duplicate monthly disability income, cancelling group coverage creates a new gap, and waiting indefinitely leaves the client exposed. The advisor can also compare benefit amounts and optional riders to create a workable compromise.
  225. The correct answer is A (Periodic reviews test whether protection still fits current income and household expenses.): A disability need changes as earnings, debt, household costs, and available workplace benefits change, so a periodic review helps test the original decision against current facts. The conversation can identify a gap, an excess, or a need to use a future-insurability provision. A one-time analysis cannot account for later life changes, automatic inflation growth requires specific contractual support, and useful reviews do not depend on replacing the insurer. Any update should also consider changes in occupation or health that may affect new coverage.
  226. The correct answer is C (Disability analysis estimates monthly income needs, while critical illness analysis estimates an immediate lump sum.): Disability planning replaces ongoing earnings during a period when work is impossible, whereas critical illness planning sizes a cash reserve for diagnosis-related costs and recovery choices. The two calculations use different time frames and therefore often produce different recommended amounts. Gross salary replacement, a mortgage-only target, and children's ages alone do not describe either full analysis. Mortgage obligations may appear in both discussions, but they serve different cash-flow purposes.
  227. The correct answer is D (Approximately sixty to seventy percent of gross pre-disability earnings across all combined sources.): Canadian insurers commonly test combined disability income against a guideline near sixty to seventy percent of gross pre-disability earnings. That target helps replace essential after-tax income without weakening the financial incentive to recover and return to work. A universal dollar benefit, full gross income replacement, and a ten to fifteen percent ceiling do not reflect typical industry practice. The insurer's actual limit, offsets, and definition govern the case even when the general range is useful.
  228. The correct answer is B (Benefits are generally received tax free because premiums were paid with after-tax personal funds.): When the insured pays disability premiums personally and receives no tax deduction for them, the resulting claim benefit generally follows after-tax funds and is received tax free. Separate group coverage does not change that result for this policy, and disability income is not automatically employment income. No general fifty percent inclusion rule applies to an individually paid disability contract. The insurer's tax reporting and the client's circumstances should still be reviewed when a claim occurs.
  229. The correct answer is B (Benefits are generally taxable to the employee as income when they are received.): Employer-paid premiums that were not reported or taxed as an employee benefit generally make resulting disability income taxable. The tax result follows the employer's untaxed funding rather than whether the employer arranged the plan. Tax-free treatment does not apply, taxability does not end after one year, and no separate flat disability rate replaces ordinary income treatment. The plan administrator's tax reporting should be reconciled with current Canada Revenue Agency guidance.
  230. The correct answer is D (Benefits generally follow the premium payer, with after-tax premiums favoring tax-free disability income.): Disability income taxation normally looks to whether premiums were paid with taxed personal funds or with employer funds that were not taxed to the employee. If the employee or owner has already borne tax on the premium, the benefit is generally received tax free, while an untaxed employer-funded premium generally creates taxable income. Neither universal taxability nor universal tax-free treatment reflects that premium-payer logic, and contract renewability does not decide the issue. Actual documentation and any premium deduction can alter the application of the general rule.
  231. The correct answer is C (The critical illness lump sum is generally received tax free by the policyholder.): A critical illness policy normally pays a tax-free living benefit once its diagnosis and survival conditions are satisfied. The payment does not replace employment income month by month in the same way disability coverage does. Half-taxation, full ordinary-income treatment, and employer contribution as the sole trigger each misstate the usual result. Tax advice should still reflect the exact policy, ownership, and current law because unusual corporate or tax structures can raise separate questions.
  232. The correct answer is A (The resulting disability benefits generally become taxable because the premiums produced a tax benefit.): If the self-employed policyholder deducted the disability premiums as a business expense, the premiums were not effectively paid with after-tax personal funds. The resulting monthly benefit generally becomes taxable to match that earlier tax treatment. A critical illness policy is a separate product, self-employed status does not override a deduction, and the claimed deduction is directly relevant. The client should retain premium and deduction records so the correct tax character can be established at claim time.
  233. The correct answer is B (Because the premium was taxed to the employee, the resulting disability benefit is generally tax free.): The employee has already included the premium value in taxable income, so that portion has effectively been paid with after-tax funds. Benefits attributable to that portion are generally not taxed again when received. The employer's role in remitting payment does not override the taxable-benefit treatment, the tax slip is directly relevant, and no general income threshold determines this result. Payroll records should document how the premium was reported.
  234. The correct answer is C (The employee-funded portion is generally tax free, while the employer-funded portion is generally taxable.): Cost-shared premiums can create cost-shared tax treatment, because the employee's after-tax contribution and the employer's untaxed contribution fund different portions of the claim. The benefit is therefore reviewed proportionally rather than treated as wholly taxable or wholly tax free. A partial employee contribution does not cleanse the employer-funded share, and a partial employer contribution does not tax the employee-funded share. Provincial tax rates may apply to taxable income, but province alone does not determine the split.
  235. The correct answer is D (Critical illness lump sums and disability income follow different tax rules tied to benefit function.): Critical illness coverage generally pays a tax-free lump sum for a covered diagnosis, while disability benefits replace ongoing income and generally follow premium funding and tax history. That difference explains why two health-related contracts can reach the same client with different tax outcomes. Age does not create the distinction, the products are not treated identically, and employer group disability benefits are often taxable when funded with untaxed employer premiums. Each product's policy wording and current tax reporting still matter.
  236. The correct answer is A (Pay the premiums personally with after-tax income instead of using an untaxed employer arrangement.): Personal after-tax premiums generally produce tax-free disability benefits because the insured has already paid tax on the money used for coverage. An unreported employer payment generally points toward taxable benefits, while benefit duration and renewability category do not create the tax result. The client should compare the higher cash cost of personal premiums with the value of tax-free claim income and any employer plan advantages. A tax professional can confirm the treatment for the exact ownership and funding arrangement.
  237. The correct answer is B (Whether a benefit is tax free or taxable changes the amount the client can actually keep.): Two contracts can state the same gross benefit while delivering different spendable income because one payment requires tax and the other does not. The advisor should compare estimated after-tax claim income with the client's essential expenses. Elimination periods and disability definitions affect access and eligibility but do not replace the net-income comparison. The premium funding history is therefore central when a client prioritizes take-home protection.
  238. The correct answer is D (The critical illness benefit is generally not taxable income and therefore does not enter those calculations.): A critical illness lump sum is generally received tax free and is not treated as annual income for income-tested program calculations based on taxable income. Program rules can vary, so the client should verify whether a benefit uses taxable income or a broader income definition. Routine inclusion, permanent loss of every program, and capital-gain reporting each misstate the usual treatment. Keeping claim documentation and asking the administering program about its exact test remains prudent.
  239. The correct answer is A (The taxable premium generally makes the resulting disability benefits tax free.): For a non-group individual disability policy, an employer payment is generally included as a taxable benefit to the employee, and the resulting benefits are generally not taxed again. The claim remains payable to the insured because she owns the policy. Renewability status does not determine this tax treatment. Payroll reporting should document the premium treatment so the insurer and client can apply the correct claim tax result.
  240. The correct answer is C (These premiums are generally not a taxable employee benefit in the common employment arrangement.): Employer-paid extended health and dental premiums are generally excluded from an employee's taxable income in ordinary Canadian employment arrangements. That treatment differs from disability premiums that fund taxable income benefits when paid by the employer without being taxed to the employee. Employers can provide these health plans, and the tax rules do not always add the premiums to income or match disability treatment exactly. Because exceptions and plan structures can vary, payroll records and current tax guidance should be checked.
  241. The correct answer is B (Disability income follows premium funding, while critical illness generally receives tax-free lump-sum treatment.): Disability benefits replace lost earnings and therefore follow whether premiums were funded with after-tax personal money. Critical illness pays a lump sum living benefit and is generally received tax free when the policy conditions are satisfied. The products are not taxed identically, their common outcomes are not reversed, and the distinction can affect both employees and self-employed claimants. Policy ownership and unusual funding structures may require separate tax review.
  242. The correct answer is D (Paying premiums personally with after-tax money generally supports a tax-free disability benefit.): Personal after-tax premium funding is the practical rule to remember because the insured has already paid tax on the coverage cost. An employer payment that is not taxed to the employee generally produces taxable benefits, while an after-tax individual premium generally supports tax-free claim income. Premium funding does matter, untaxed employer payment does not guarantee exemption, and properly funded individual disability benefits can also be tax free. The exact result still requires review of payroll and deduction records.
  243. The correct answer is C (The plan's non-evidence maximum allows enrollment without individual medical evidence at that level.): Group plans often spread risk across the employee group and permit automatic coverage up to a stated non-evidence maximum. A request within that ceiling can therefore be enrolled without individual medical questions. Evidence may be required above the limit, the employer does not conduct a secret individual assessment, and group long-term disability coverage is a common product. The plan text defines both eligibility and the amount that triggers evidence.
  244. The correct answer is A (Evidence of insurability, such as health questions or additional medical information.): Coverage above a group plan's non-evidence maximum usually requires the employee to show that the added risk is acceptable. That evidence may include a health questionnaire, medical records, or an examination requested by the insurer. A prior claim history, dental fee guide, and unchanged job title do not substitute for evidence of insurability. The employee should answer accurately and provide requested records within the plan's deadline because approval of the higher amount depends on that assessment.
  245. The correct answer is A (The employer carries claim costs while the insurer provides administrative services for a fee.): In an administrative services only arrangement, the employer funds the plan's actual claim costs and bears the risk of higher-than-expected claims. The insurer or administrator processes claims and performs plan duties for a service fee. Claim risk does not shift automatically to the insurer, employees do not personally fund the employer's excess costs in the described model, and these arrangements can cover more than dental benefits. Funding reserves and stop-loss protection are separate design choices the employer must review.
  246. The correct answer is D (Whether the group contract includes a conversion privilege and she applies within its deadline.): A conversion privilege can allow eligible group members to obtain specified individual coverage without fresh evidence after group coverage ends. Availability depends on the contract's eligibility rules, benefit limits, and strict application window. Personal premium share, a prior disability claim, and another employer's plan do not create conversion rights. The departing member should request the conversion terms before coverage ends because missing the deadline can forfeit the no-evidence opportunity.
  247. The correct answer is B (Group critical illness coverage often ends or reduces when employment with the sponsor ends.): Group critical illness protection usually depends on membership in the covered employee group, so termination, retirement, or reduced hours can affect continuation. An individually owned policy generally remains in force while the owner pays its premiums and follows its terms. Benefit size, exclusion of core conditions from every group contract, and full individual evidence for every employee are not reliable distinctions. The employee should review continuation or conversion options before employment ends.
  248. The correct answer is C (It provides documentation showing that the disability meets the policy's claim conditions.): Proof of claim lets the insurer verify the medical condition, date of disability, treatment, restrictions, and effect on the claimant's ability to work. Those facts determine whether the policy definition and other claim conditions are satisfied. The forms do not change renewability status, determine a dental fee guide, or waive the elimination period. Complete, consistent records help the insurer assess the claim and reduce avoidable delays.
  249. The correct answer is C (It confirms that the claimant continues to satisfy the policy's disability definition.): Long-term disability payments usually depend on continuing disability, so the insurer may request updated medical evidence during an open claim. The report helps determine whether work capacity or treatment has changed and whether payment should continue under the contract. Conversion eligibility, critical illness survival, and a repeated elimination period address different rules. The claimant should provide current medical information and review the policy's ongoing-proof and appeal provisions if a decision is disputed.
  250. The correct answer is B (The claimant must survive the policy's specified survival period following diagnosis.): A critical illness contract typically makes the lump sum payable only after the insured survives a stated period from diagnosis. Medical proof establishes the condition, but the survival condition and other policy requirements must also be satisfied. A separate disability claim, exhaustion of a monthly benefit period, and an occupation change are not normal prerequisites. The claimant should confirm the exact diagnosis date, condition definition, and survival-period wording in the policy.

Practice Test 2 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is D (She should request a formal review and submit additional medical evidence for reconsideration.): A formal review lets the insurer reconsider the claim with fuller medical evidence while the policy remains active. Surrendering the contract addresses ownership rather than the reason the insurer denied the claim. Many insurers maintain internal review or complaint channels after an initial claim decision. Buying different coverage does not resolve the existing claim or preserve its submitted evidence.
  2. The correct answer is A (Claimant authorization is required before physicians release confidential medical records.): Written authorization supports privacy requirements and gives the insurer a lawful route to necessary specialist records. An insurer's role in handling a claim does not remove the need to respect confidential health information. Renewability wording affects premium and cancellation guarantees rather than consent to release records. Disability claim investigations commonly rely on medical evidence obtained with valid authorization.
  3. The correct answer is C (Dollar cost averaging, which spreads equal purchases across changing unit prices.): Fixed contributions buy varying unit counts and avoid a decision based on forecasting the best price. Market timing tries to identify favorable entry points rather than committing at regular intervals. Asset allocation concerns the portfolio mix rather than the timing of identical deposits. Rebalancing restores target weights and does not describe a recurring purchase schedule.
  4. The correct answer is A (A long investment horizon that leaves earned growth inside the plan.): Compounding produces stronger results when earned growth remains invested over many years. A brief holding period reduces the time available for repeated compounding. Frequent withdrawals remove capital that would otherwise support subsequent growth. The length of the investment period supports compounding more directly than a fixed nominal rate.
  5. The correct answer is B (Diversification, which spreads savings across asset classes to limit one holding's influence.): Spreading savings across asset classes reduces the effect of one sector on the whole portfolio. Leverage increases exposure by borrowing and does not describe spreading investments. Speculation concentrates exposure in a forecast rather than reducing dependence on one sector. Arbitrage exploits price differences and does not describe a balanced portfolio mix.
  6. The correct answer is D (Liquidity, meaning how quickly the holding converts to cash without material loss.): Funds required soon should remain accessible without a forced sale during poor market conditions. Past returns describe historical performance rather than the client's immediate access requirement. Tax planning can affect after tax value, but near term availability remains the primary constraint. Active or passive management changes how the portfolio is run, not when the client can obtain cash.
  7. The correct answer is A (Growth remains tax deferred inside the plan and becomes taxable when withdrawn.): RRSP assets are not taxed while they remain in plan, and withdrawals enter taxable income when taken. Tax free treatment after withdrawal belongs to a TFSA rather than a regular RRSP withdrawal. Annual accrual taxation describes a non registered account rather than the deferred RRSP structure. RRSP withdrawals are generally included in income instead of receiving capital gains treatment.
  8. The correct answer is B (TFSA withdrawals are excluded from income because contributions use after tax dollars.): TFSA withdrawals do not enter taxable income because contributions use after tax money. A flat withholding tax applies to certain registered withdrawals, not normal TFSA payments. The TFSA and RRSP systems maintain separate contribution room rules and withdrawal effects. TFSA tax free treatment does not depend on a ten year holding requirement.
  9. The correct answer is C (A prescribed minimum payment must be withdrawn and included in annual income.): A RRIF requires a prescribed minimum annual payment and the amount is included in income. The minimum begins under the prescribed rules and does not wait until age ninety. RRIF payments have a prescribed minimum rather than a fixed age independent maximum cap. A RRIF generally does not accept new contributions after the RRSP has been converted.
  10. The correct answer is D (Higher potential returns generally come with a corresponding increase in investment risk.): Greater return potential usually compensates the investor for accepting more uncertainty and possible loss. Guaranteed products favor predictability and do not promise superior performance over long periods. Risk and return are connected because investors generally demand compensation for uncertainty. Higher risk can produce losses as easily as higher returns, so greater return is not guaranteed.
  11. The correct answer is C (Inflation risk, because rising prices can reduce the balance's future purchasing power.): Rising consumer prices reduce how much the fixed balance can purchase over time. Currency risk concerns exchange rate changes and does not explain a domestic account's lost purchasing power. Credit risk concerns issuer default rather than gradual loss from changes in the costs of goods. Liquidity risk concerns access to money and does not describe erosion caused by rising prices.
  12. The correct answer is A (Volatility, which describes how often and how sharply the fund's value moves.): Volatility describes the scale and frequency of value changes experienced by the fund. Duration measures interest rate sensitivity in fixed income portfolios rather than general price movement. Yield describes income production relative to price and does not measure the pattern of price changes. Tracking error compares returns with a benchmark instead of describing the fund's own value swings.
  13. The correct answer is D (A longer time horizon provides more opportunity to recover before the money is needed.): A long horizon creates time for recovery before withdrawals are needed during retirement. Regulatory asset mix rules do not require younger clients to hold greater volatility. Short term fluctuation remains relevant because a decline can affect near term purchasing goals. Retirees may hold market investments when their circumstances and risk capacity support that choice.
  14. The correct answer is B (It provides an emergency reserve that covers unexpected costs without selling investments.): An emergency reserve covers near term costs while long term investments remain invested. A reserve reflects prudent planning rather than a regulatory condition for issuing the contract. The reserve does not change the guarantee percentage stated in the insurance contract. Know your client information remains necessary even when the client keeps accessible savings.
  15. The correct answer is C (Gather finances, objectives, time horizon, and risk tolerance before recommending.): Suitability begins with documented finances, goals, time horizon, and risk tolerance. A low fee can help the client, but cost alone does not establish that the fund fits the profile. Commission compensation does not determine the suitability of a recommended segregated fund. Objectives must be gathered before the transaction and cannot be documented only after the purchase.
  16. The correct answer is A (Risk tolerance reflects attitude while risk capacity reflects ability to absorb a loss.): Suitability considers both the client's willingness to accept risk and the financial ability to absorb loss. The concepts differ because a confident attitude does not enlarge the client's actual financial capacity. Stated comfort is relevant, but it must be reconciled with debts, income, and essential spending needs. Risk capacity depends on the complete financial picture rather than age alone.
  17. The correct answer is D (Confirm the product is suitable for the client's documented objectives and circumstances.): The documented profile provides the standard against which the fund's fees, risks, and features are judged. Commission targets create a conflict and do not establish that the recommendation suits the client. Market share says little about whether the product matches the client's time horizon and risk capacity. Familiarity from adviser training does not provide evidence of suitability for the client.
  18. The correct answer is B (The stated objective, risk tolerance, and time horizon jointly guide fund category selection.): The three details form the client profile used to compare objectives, expected returns, and tolerable declines. Age can inform planning, but it does not replace objectives, risk tolerance, or the stated horizon. Recent performance alone cannot establish suitability because markets change and past results do not predict future returns. Signing an application does not remove the need to match the product to documented client needs.
  19. The correct answer is A (Update current finances in the know your client file and reassess suitability.): A material change can alter both the client's objectives and ability to absorb loss, so the file needs current information. A previously accurate form can become stale after inherited assets and new debt change the financial picture. Know your client obligations continue through the relationship and are not limited to a first purchase. Waiting through a substantial change risks an unsuitable recommendation before the next scheduled review.
  20. The correct answer is B (A shorter horizon leaves less time to recover from a downturn before withdrawals begin.): A five year horizon creates limited recovery time if equities decline shortly before the purchase. Equity funds can be available to suitable clients, and eligibility depends on documented circumstances rather than an outright horizon ban. Risk tolerance affects the recommendation, but time horizon still governs when the money must be available. The horizon alone does not set the guarantee percentage, which follows the specific contract terms.
  21. The correct answer is C (Explain the concern in writing, document the client's decision, and avoid an unsuitable sale.): Explaining the conflict, documenting the discussion, and declining the unsuitable transaction protects the client and records the advice. Client choice does not remove the adviser's suitability obligation when the documented profile conflicts with the product. Ending the relationship without an explanation avoids the required discussion and record of the concern. Changing recorded answers would falsify the profile and conceal the actual suitability conflict.
  22. The correct answer is D (An ongoing process reviewed and updated as the client's circumstances change.): Client information remains relevant throughout the relationship because finances, goals, and obligations can change. One completed form cannot support later recommendations after income, debts, and family needs have changed. Referral marketing is unrelated to the suitability information required for a fund recommendation. The process applies beyond a first purchase and should respond to material changes as they occur.
  23. The correct answer is D (It estimates how much investment risk the client's finances can support.): Income, assets, and debt establish the client's practical ability to withstand investment losses. The suitability profile is unrelated to calculating the adviser's expected commission. Guarantee percentages follow contract terms and are not set by the client's net worth or debt level. Investment suitability still requires the same financial information even when no loan application exists.
  24. The correct answer is A (The documented short horizon and capital preservation objective for the planned purchase.): Money needed in eighteen months should prioritize stability and access over the possibility of stronger long term growth. Historical performance is useful context but does not override the client's stated need to preserve capital. An adviser's short term market forecast does not replace documented objectives and time horizon. Popularity among other clients says little about suitability for this client's planned purchase.
  25. The correct answer is B (A stated percentage of deposits is payable when the contract reaches its maturity date.): The maturity guarantee returns a contracted percentage of deposits when the contract reaches its stated maturity. Market value can fall below deposits before maturity, so the guarantee is not a daily price floor. The feature does not promise a fixed annual return while the fund remains invested. Withdrawals before maturity are handled at market value rather than receiving the full maturity protection.
  26. The correct answer is C (The death benefit guarantee, which pays a minimum percentage of deposits on death.): The death benefit guarantee pays at least the contracted percentage of deposits if the annuitant dies before maturity. The maturity guarantee responds at the stated maturity date and does not address an earlier death. A reset option can raise the protected base, but it is not the guarantee triggered by death. The management expense ratio is an ongoing cost and does not supply the death benefit payment.
  27. The correct answer is D (A reset option, which raises the guaranteed base to the current market value.): A reset raises the protected base to a current market value so later declines are measured from that higher amount. A surrender charge is a withdrawal cost and does not preserve an increased guarantee. A prescribed annuity designation changes payment taxation rather than the protected fund value. Asset segregation protects the fund structure from general insurer claims and does not reset the guarantee.
  28. The correct answer is B (Many contracts specify a maximum age after which the reset option ends.): Insurers commonly set an age ceiling for resets, so the contract wording controls later eligibility. Reset rights can end before death and are not available at every age under standard terms. Many contracts allow repeated resets subject to limits rather than one lifetime exercise. The feature can be available across adulthood and is not limited to clients under thirty.
  29. The correct answer is C (The maturity date commonly moves later so the new guarantee completes its holding period.): A reset usually restarts the guarantee period, so the new protected amount matures after a later date. The original date can move after a reset, so it does not remain unchanged. A reset adjusts protection while the fund investment normally continues rather than terminating the contract. The new guarantee commonly requires a longer holding period and does not accelerate maturity.
  30. The correct answer is A (The one hundred percent guarantee contract generally carries a higher MER for stronger protection.): Stronger guarantees require greater insurer reserves, so the one hundred percent feature generally carries a higher fee. The guarantee levels create different insurance costs, so identical MERs are not the expected result. Guarantee strength affects the cost of the insurance wrapper even when the portfolios look similar. A higher fee does not promise a higher net return because the insurance cost and investment performance serve different purposes.
  31. The correct answer is B (The guarantee does not cover an early withdrawal, so the market value applies.): A partial withdrawal before maturity is paid at market value, and the guarantee remains tied to the contract's payment event. The maturity guarantee applies at maturity rather than to each withdrawal along the way. The insurer does not restore an early withdrawal to the original deposit amount. A partial withdrawal can reduce the guaranteed base proportionally, but it does not eliminate the remaining protection.
  32. The correct answer is A (Market value rises and falls daily while the guarantee affects payment at maturity or death.): The contract's market value can fall below deposits while the maturity or death guarantee provides a payment floor. The guarantee is not a daily unit price floor, so declines appear on normal account statements. The insurer does not top up the account after each daily loss because protection is tested at a contract event. The fund remains invested rather than converting to cash when value falls below the guaranteed amount.
  33. The correct answer is D (An automatic reset triggers on a scheduled date or condition without a client request.): An automatic reset is applied by the contract's schedule or condition without a separate client instruction. Automatic terms can apply to maturity protection as well as death benefits, depending on the contract. Automatic timing is defined by contract rules rather than arbitrary insurer selection. The automatic mechanism is administrative and does not establish a greater guarantee percentage than a manual reset.
  34. The correct answer is C (The client waits for the next permitted period before locking in additional gains.): The reset limit restricts the number of elections available during the permitted period. Reaching the annual maximum does not eliminate the guarantee already established. An adviser cannot override a contractual reset limit through a service request. The contract remains invested, so a maximum reset count does not trigger automatic liquidation.
  35. The correct answer is A (A reset changes the protected amount while the fund's regular management fees continue.): A reset protects a higher amount while the management expense ratio continues to apply. The management fee remains part of the fund's ongoing cost structure after a reset. A reset changes the guarantee rather than creating a proportional reduction in management fees. The insurer does not assume the client's management fee because a reset was elected.
  36. The correct answer is C (A reset after strong growth protects the elevated value before a possible market decline.): A reset after growth protects the risen account value as the new guarantee floor. A downturn generally produces a lower protected value when market value has fallen. Timing matters because the reset uses the fund's market value when the election applies. Many contracts permit resets during growth periods and do not reserve them for downturns.
  37. The correct answer is D (Fees reduce market value, but the guarantee is based on stated deposits less withdrawals.): Ongoing management expenses reduce the fund's market value throughout the contract term. The maturity guarantee is normally based on a stated percentage of deposits, adjusted for withdrawals and any contractual reset. Fees can therefore make the guarantee more valuable without reducing the deposit based protected amount. They are not refunded, paused, or charged only when the contract is surrendered.
  38. The correct answer is B (The policyowner, the annuitant, and the beneficiary in the insurance contract.): A segregated fund contract identifies the policyowner, annuitant, and beneficiary roles. Mutual fund dealers and securities regulators do not replace the three insurance roles named in the contract. A bank trustee and estate executor do not define the insurance contract's ownership and life measurement roles. Adviser and compliance roles support the sale but are not the contract's three insurance parties.
  39. The correct answer is D (A qualifying family beneficiary may protect proceeds from creditors, subject to provincial law.): Eligible family beneficiary designations can support creditor protection at death while provincial insurance law and the facts affect the result. Creditor protection may be available when a qualifying spouse is named, so a blanket denial is inaccurate. Naming the policyowner as beneficiary generally weakens the family protection analysis rather than supporting it. A designation change can alter the protection analysis, but it does not automatically eliminate the feature under every contract.
  40. The correct answer is A (The policyowner needs beneficiary consent for changes affecting the protected interest.): An irrevocable designation gives the beneficiary a protected interest that generally requires consent before changes affecting that interest. The policyowner cannot freely replace an irrevocable beneficiary without the required consent. Irrevocability concerns beneficiary rights and does not alter the guarantee percentage in the contract. A policyowner remains necessary and manages the contract subject to the protected beneficiary interest.
  41. The correct answer is C (The proceeds pass directly to the named beneficiary and generally avoid probate.): A named beneficiary can receive the death benefit directly, which generally avoids estate administration and probate delays. The proceeds are not redirected to a charity unless the client selected that beneficiary. Government custody does not normally follow a valid beneficiary designation. Estate distribution occurs when the estate is named or no valid beneficiary designation applies.
  42. The correct answer is B (The insurer owns the assets while the client holds units linked to fund performance.): The insurer holds legal title to the segregated fund assets while the client owns units tied to performance. The client does not receive direct title to each individual security held inside the insurer's fund. The adviser does not acquire joint ownership merely by selling the segregated fund contract. The client's units do not transfer the underlying portfolio to the estate immediately upon purchase.
  43. The correct answer is D (It protects fund assets from claims related to the insurer's unrelated business operations.): The separate account structure helps shield the fund assets from claims connected with the insurer's unrelated operations. Segregation addresses legal separation and does not promise superior investment returns. A separate account does not exempt the contract from management expenses. The fund remains exposed to ordinary market movements despite the legal separation from general assets.
  44. The correct answer is C (The annuitant's life measures contract events while the policyowner controls contract rights.): The annuitant defines the life used for maturity and death benefit events, while the policyowner holds contract rights. The two roles can be held by one person, so separate individuals are not required. The annuitant role exists before maturity and remains central to contract events. The policyowner role is part of the insurance contract structure and is not limited to sales outside Quebec.
  45. The correct answer is B (Many contracts allow successor owner or annuitant designations, while incapacity requires separate authority.): Successor owner or successor annuitant provisions can preserve contract control after the owner dies without ending the contract. Contracts can also include more than one named beneficiary and separate successor designations. Any successor provision does not automatically replace the death benefit beneficiary. Incapacity must be addressed separately under a valid power of attorney or other authority recognized for the contract.
  46. The correct answer is A (The segregated fund MER includes insurance guarantees unavailable through the mutual fund.): The insurance wrapper adds guarantee costs, which generally makes the segregated fund MER higher. A legal rule does not require every segregated fund fee to exceed every mutual fund fee. A higher MER is a cost drag and does not promise a stronger net return. Mutual funds also carry portfolio management costs, while the segregated contract adds insurance features.
  47. The correct answer is B (It typically combines portfolio management and guarantee costs within the ongoing MER.): The MER commonly reflects portfolio management, distribution, administration, and insurance guarantee costs. Adviser compensation can be part of the fee, but it does not represent the complete MER. Government taxes can apply separately and do not define the MER's operating cost structure. Reset activity does not create a single fixed fee included in each contract's ongoing MER.
  48. The correct answer is C (It is deducted from the fund's assets whether unit values rise or fall.): The management expense ratio is charged against fund assets even when market performance is negative. Positive returns are not a condition for the ongoing management fee. A market decline does not create an automatic fee refund. When value falls below the guarantee, the ongoing fee structure continues rather than pausing.
  49. The correct answer is D (A small fee gap compounds over decades and reduces the final account balance.): A persistent fee gap reduces assets available for compounding, so its effect grows over a long holding period. The impact extends beyond the year charged because smaller assets earn less future growth. Performance differences do not make a small fee gap irrelevant over decades. The guarantee percentage follows contract terms rather than rising automatically with the fee.
  50. The correct answer is A (A deferred sales charge applies to an early withdrawal within the stated surrender period.): Under a legacy contract, a deferred sales charge reduces proceeds when the client withdraws during the schedule's early years. New deferred sales charge sales were expected to cease by June 1, 2023, so the feature should not be presented as available on a current contract. The charge is generally separate from ongoing fund expenses and is not deducted before the purchase amount is invested. The surrender schedule controls any remaining charge on the legacy units.
  51. The correct answer is A (A 100% maturity guarantee generally carries a higher management expense ratio than a 75% guarantee): A stronger maturity guarantee increases the insurer's reserve and hedging costs, so the 100% protection typically carries the higher fee. Assuming the guarantee level has little effect or that both guarantees carry the same ratio ignores that added protection cost. Attributing the fee difference mainly to fund class also misses the specific guarantee feature being compared. The client should compare the disclosed cost of each guarantee level before selecting the contract.
  52. The correct answer is C (Compare the management expense ratios along with any applicable sales charges disclosed for each product): Management expense ratios capture ongoing fund costs while disclosed sales charges reveal the transaction costs that affect the client's net result. A comparison centered on trailing commissions does not identify every applicable sales charge. A representative's service record is unrelated to product cost, and removing sales charges from the calculation produces an incomplete picture. The disclosed measures should be reviewed together before the product is selected.
  53. The correct answer is D (A life annuity purchased without a guarantee period or estate refund feature): A straight life annuity uses the entire premium to support lifetime income, so removing refund promises ordinarily produces the highest periodic payment. A term certain contract ends after its fixed period and does not directly solve longevity risk. A joint contract covers a second life that Marcel does not need, while a deferred contract postpones the income he now wants. Marcel's acceptance of no estate refund makes the straight life design suitable.
  54. The correct answer is B (A term certain annuity that guarantees payments for a fixed period): Denise's contract promises scheduled income for exactly 20 years and sends any remaining payments to her beneficiary if she dies early. A single life annuity depends on Denise's lifespan rather than a fixed term. A joint contract covers two lives, and an impaired annuity reflects health-based mortality pricing rather than the stated payment period. The fixed 20 year promise is the defining feature here.
  55. The correct answer is B (A joint and last survivor annuity with a reduction on first death): This design continues income until the second spouse dies and permits a lower survivor payment after the first death. A deferred joint contract that pays only one person does not protect both lives as described. A term certain annuity ends after ten years, and a single life contract on Harold stops at his death. The survivor reduction lowers cost while preserving income for the remaining spouse.
  56. The correct answer is A (Immediate contracts usually begin income soon after purchase while deferred contracts postpone payments): The primary distinction is when the payout phase starts, with immediate contracts beginning soon and deferred contracts accumulating toward a later date. Registered or non-registered status can apply to either contract and is not the timing distinction. Tax treatment also does not define the two designs in the way described, and an immediate annuity is normally an income product rather than a lump sum payment. The timing of the first payment is the reliable feature to review.
  57. The correct answer is C (Prescribed taxation spreads taxable income evenly while non-prescribed taxation recognizes more interest early): Prescribed taxation averages the capital and interest elements to produce a relatively level taxable amount for each payment. Non-prescribed taxation follows the declining principal balance, so more interest is generally taxed in the earlier years. Capital gains reporting and a registered-only scope do not describe prescribed annuity treatment. The reversed timing statement also confuses which method produces the level taxable portion.
  58. The correct answer is D (The remaining scheduled payments continue to the named beneficiary for the balance): The guarantee period protects the beneficiary by requiring payment for the portion remaining after the annuitant dies. Six years remain under the ten year minimum, so those scheduled payments continue. A guarantee does not convert the contract into a return of the original premium, nor does it authorize a replacement annuitant. The beneficiary receives the promised payment stream under the contract terms.
  59. The correct answer is A (An impaired or enhanced annuity priced using his shortened life expectancy): Medical underwriting recognizes a shorter expected payout period and can support a higher payment for the same premium. A joint contract changes the number of lives covered rather than applying impaired mortality. A term certain contract changes the payment period, and a deferred contract changes when payments begin. The described medical evidence and higher quote identify impaired or enhanced annuity pricing.
  60. The correct answer is C (Most life annuity contracts cannot be commuted for a lump sum after income payments begin): Once a life annuity annuitizes, the insurer has begun pooling longevity risk, so surrender or commutation is generally unavailable without a special provision. A client cannot ordinarily demand the remaining value as cash, and the contract does not provide an automatic premium refund with a penalty. The restriction applies to life annuity payouts rather than leaving them unrestricted. Liquidity needs should therefore be planned before conversion.
  61. The correct answer is B (A life annuity guards against longevity risk while term certain income ends after a fixed period): Life annuity payments continue for the annuitant's lifetime and therefore directly address the risk of outliving savings. A term certain annuity pays for a set number of years whether or not the annuitant survives, so it can end while the retiree is still living. Reversing the roles, treating both as lifetime products, or treating both as fixed term products misstates their core designs. The lifetime promise is the feature that manages longevity risk.
  62. The correct answer is D (A deferred annuity that postpones the start of income to a later date): A deferred annuity allows the client to purchase now and set the payout phase to begin near retirement. An immediate contract would start income much sooner than the client needs. Joint coverage changes the lives protected, and impaired pricing reflects health evidence rather than deliberate income deferral. The deferral feature directly matches the ten year gap between purchase and desired income.
  63. The correct answer is C (Because the insurer plans for payments over a potentially longer combined lifespan): A joint and last survivor contract can continue until the longer of two lives ends, so the same premium normally supports a lower periodic payment. Higher income tax and a regulatory cap do not explain the actuarial price difference. A ten year guarantee is an optional feature rather than the reason every joint contract pays less. The longer expected payment horizon is the controlling pricing factor.
  64. The correct answer is B (Each payment splits into a taxable interest part and an untaxed capital part): A non-registered annuity returns the client's after-tax capital while also paying taxable interest on that capital. Treating the entire payment as tax free ignores the interest component, and delaying all taxation to age seventy one confuses annuity reporting with RRSP maturity rules. Capital gains treatment also does not describe ordinary annuity interest. Each payment therefore contains both taxable income and a return of capital.
  65. The correct answer is D (Older buyers have a shorter expected payout period, allowing higher periodic payments): Annuity pricing depends heavily on expected remaining lifetime, so the same premium can support a higher monthly amount over fewer expected years. Age does not automatically raise market interest rates or create an administration fee discount. A longer contribution period also does not explain the higher quote paid to the older client. The shorter anticipated payout period is the primary reason for the difference.
  66. The correct answer is A (A guarantee period added on top of an underlying life annuity): The life annuity provides income for the client's lifetime while the guarantee ensures at least five years of payments continue to her beneficiary if death occurs early. A term certain arrangement without a life contingency would not provide lifetime income. A joint designation would make the son's lifespan part of the annuity payout, which is different from a five year guarantee. Impaired pricing reflects health underwriting rather than beneficiary protection.
  67. The correct answer is A (It is based mainly on years of Canadian residence rather than an employment history): Old Age Security is financed through general federal revenue and its basic eligibility is tied mainly to residence and age. Employment hours, CPP contributions, or a private employer pension do not determine basic OAS eligibility. Past payroll contributions describe CPP financing rather than the residence-based OAS program. A client should therefore review residence history separately from contributory pension records.
  68. The correct answer is D (An income-tested recovery that reduces OAS after net income exceeds a threshold): The OAS recovery tax is calculated through the income tax system and increases when calculated net income passes the annual threshold. It is not a one-time retirement charge, a permanent work-related suspension, or a voluntary repayment plan available at every income level. The reduction depends on annual income testing. An advisor should connect the notice to the client's net income and applicable recovery threshold.
  69. The correct answer is B (Her contributory history and pensionable earnings during her working years): CPP retirement benefit calculations are based on the client's contributions to the plan and pensionable earnings over the contributory period. RRSP balances and marital status do not determine the basic CPP retirement amount. Residence history belongs mainly to OAS eligibility rather than the CPP earnings test. A CPP estimate should therefore begin with the client's contribution record.
  70. The correct answer is C (The CPP disability benefit paid to eligible severely disabled contributors): This benefit is designed to replace part of the earnings of an eligible contributor who has a severe and prolonged disability. A retirement pension requires the client to be at or near pensionable age, while survivor and death benefits arise from another person's death. The client's circumstances concern disability before retirement rather than any of those events. Eligibility still depends on the CPP disability contribution requirements.
  71. The correct answer is B (The CPP survivor's pension paid monthly to the surviving spouse): A survivor's pension can provide ongoing monthly income based on the deceased contributor's CPP record when the survivor meets the eligibility conditions. Disability benefits concern the living contributor's severe and prolonged disability. The death benefit is a one-time payment to the estate or eligible person, while the deceased person's retirement pension cannot remain registered for monthly payments to the widow. The widow's question points specifically to the survivor benefit.
  72. The correct answer is A (It lets spouses both receiving CPP redistribute part of their pension income for tax planning): Pension sharing allocates part of a CPP retirement pension between spouses so the combined income may be taxed more evenly. It does not create an additional combined benefit or permanently transfer the entire entitlement. Sharing can be changed or cease under the applicable rules rather than operating as an irreversible transfer. The provision concerns both spouses' CPP retirement pensions, not survivor benefits alone.
  73. The correct answer is D (OAS relies mainly on residence and general revenue while CPP is contribution-based): Old Age Security uses age and Canadian residence for basic eligibility and is financed from general revenue. The CPP connects benefits to pensionable earnings and contributions made by workers and employers. Reversing those structures, treating both programs as contribution-funded, or assuming identical formulas ignores their separate legal bases. Receiving both benefits therefore requires two different eligibility reviews.
  74. The correct answer is C (It is an added monthly benefit for low income recipients of Old Age Security): The Guaranteed Income Supplement provides ongoing monthly support to qualifying OAS recipients whose income is low. It does not replace OAS, arrive as a one-time payment, or depend mainly on CPP pensionable earnings. It is also a retirement benefit for OAS recipients rather than a pre-retirement disability payment. Eligibility requires the client to apply and satisfy the applicable income test.
  75. The correct answer is C (Contributions are generally deductible while investment growth inside the plan is tax deferred): An eligible RRSP contribution can reduce taxable income within the available deduction room, and growth is normally sheltered until withdrawal. Moving the deduction to the withdrawal date or taxing annual growth as capital gains misstates RRSP mechanics. Non-deductible contributions with tax-free withdrawals describe a TFSA characteristic rather than an RRSP. The client should confirm contribution room before using the deduction.
  76. The correct answer is A (A spousal RRSP registered in the wife's name but funded using the husband's deduction room): A spousal RRSP allows the contributing spouse to use available deduction room while the receiving spouse owns the plan and may later draw taxable retirement income. A locked-in account holds pension transfers rather than new spousal contributions. A RRIF is an income vehicle rather than a contribution arrangement, and a group RRSP is organized through an employer. The spousal structure directly supports retirement income splitting.
  77. The correct answer is B (Convert the RRSP into a registered retirement income fund or an annuity): Maturity requires the RRSP funds to move into a qualifying income arrangement such as a RRIF or an annuity. A spousal RRSP is a contribution plan rather than a way to avoid maturity, and opening or retaining an ordinary RRSP does not solve the year-end requirement. The RRIF or annuity keeps the funds tax deferred while starting a prescribed withdrawal or income schedule. The client should compare withdrawal flexibility and fees before choosing.
  78. The correct answer is D (A minimum amount tied to age must be withdrawn and taxed as income each year): RRIF rules require an annual minimum based on the holder's age and account value, and the withdrawn amount is included in taxable income. The minimum is not optional, and a single dollar maximum does not apply to every holder. RRIF withdrawals retain their tax-deferred origin only until the funds leave the plan. The client should use the prescribed factor and the year-end account value to plan cash flow.
  79. The correct answer is C (Unused TFSA contribution room generally carries into future years without expiry): Every eligible resident accumulates annual TFSA room, and unused space remains available for later use. Withdrawals do not generally reduce future room in the manner described, and the room does not stop growing after the opening year. TFSA room is not based on earned income like RRSP deduction room. The client should track cumulative room separately from RRSP limits.
  80. The correct answer is B (The withdrawn amount is added back to contribution room at the start of the following year): A TFSA withdrawal generally creates replacement room on January 1 of the next calendar year rather than immediately. A ten year reset and a requirement to close the account are not part of the TFSA room calculation. The client must compare the replacement contribution with room available at the time it is made. Re-contributing in the same year can therefore create an excess contribution risk.
  81. The correct answer is A (A registered education savings plan opened for the named beneficiary): An RESP is designed for family education deposits and can attract federal education grants that a TFSA cannot provide. A registered disability savings plan requires disability tax credit eligibility and addresses disability savings. A registered retirement savings plan belongs to the contributor and is designed for retirement income, not a child's post-secondary grant incentives. The RESP's subscriber, beneficiary, and contribution rules make it the suitable structure.
  82. The correct answer is D (The subscriber opens and funds the plan while the beneficiary receives education payments): The subscriber establishes the RESP and makes contributions, while the beneficiary is the student named to receive educational assistance payments when eligible. Contributor, settlor, trustee, policyholder, annuitant, and survivor are terms drawn from other account or insurance structures. The RESP roles describe a funding owner and a named student recipient. These labels should be used correctly when explaining ownership and payments.
  83. The correct answer is B (The grant matches a portion of eligible contributions within stated annual and lifetime limits): The Canada Education Savings Grant adds a percentage-based amount to qualifying RESP contributions, subject to annual and lifetime caps. It is not a fixed payment unrelated to deposits, a replacement for family saving, or a student loan. The exact match depends on the eligible contribution and available grant room. The parent can therefore use the grant limit to plan each year's deposit.
  84. The correct answer is C (A registered disability savings plan designed for a qualifying beneficiary): A registered disability savings plan is the registered vehicle associated with a beneficiary who qualifies for the Disability Tax Credit. A retirement income fund and a locked-in retirement account address retirement money rather than disability savings. A deferred profit sharing plan is an employer plan funded from business profits. The Disability Tax Credit link is the identifying feature of the registered disability savings plan.
  85. The correct answer is A (The Canada Disability Savings Grant and the Canada Disability Savings Bond): The Canada Disability Savings Grant responds to qualifying family contributions, while the Canada Disability Savings Bond supports eligible low income beneficiaries. OAS supplements and CPP benefits are retirement or contributory benefits rather than RDSP incentives. The education grant and learning bond belong to the RESP system, not the disability savings system. The two correctly named programs perform the two distinct funding roles described.
  86. The correct answer is D (TFSA withdrawals are generally not taxed while RRSP withdrawals are included in taxable income): A TFSA uses after-tax contributions and normally permits tax-free withdrawals, while RRSP contributions were generally deductible and withdrawals are taxed. Reversing the accounts, taxing both, or reversing the contribution treatment misstates their tax design. The source of the contribution affects how the withdrawal is handled. The client should compare after-tax cash needs rather than account balances alone.
  87. The correct answer is D (Unused RRSP deduction room generally carries forward into future years): Eligible earned income creates RRSP room, pension adjustments reduce it, and unused space remains available for later contributions. It does not expire merely because the year ends, and earned income is a major part of the calculation. Dedicated Home Buyers' Plan and lifelong learning provisions do not replace ordinary carried-forward deduction room. The client should confirm the Canada Revenue Agency room figure before contributing.
  88. The correct answer is B (The grant is repaid to the government while original personal contributions can be returned): Grant money was paid for a qualifying education beneficiary and generally must be returned when the plan is closed without qualifying educational use. The subscriber's own contributions are not grant money and can ordinarily be withdrawn. Simply leaving grant assets in a closed plan or assuming they transfer with earnings misstates the treatment. Available family or transfer options should still be reviewed before closure.
  89. The correct answer is A (A defined benefit pension plan promising a formula based retirement income): A defined benefit plan calculates the promised pension from factors such as salary history and years of service. A defined contribution plan instead depends on accumulated deposits and investment results. A group RRSP consists of individual registered accounts, and a deferred profit sharing plan depends on employer profits. The salary-and-service formula identifies the first offer as a defined benefit pension.
  90. The correct answer is C (The employee bears investment risk in defined contribution while the employer bears it in defined benefit): In a defined contribution plan, account investment results affect the employee's retirement fund, while a defined benefit plan requires the employer to fund the promised benefit. The government does not absorb ordinary employer plan investment risk. Treating both plan types as placing risk equally on one party ignores their different guarantees. This risk allocation is a central comparison between the two job offers.
  91. The correct answer is A (Vesting of employer contributions in the employee's own name after service): Vesting is the point at which the employee gains a legal right to employer-funded pension benefits earned through service. Commutation converts a benefit into a lump sum, portability concerns transfers between plans, and locking-in restricts later access. Those concepts may affect the pension after vesting but do not name the ownership right itself. The service-based entitlement described in the stem is vesting.
  92. The correct answer is C (It restricts early cash withdrawal so the funds remain earmarked for retirement income): Locking-in protects pension money by limiting access to a lump sum or other early use and keeping it available for retirement income. The rules do not pin funds to one institution, stop employer contributions for active members, or force a government bond allocation. Investment choices remain subject to the receiving plan's rules. The central restriction is the preservation of the funds for their retirement purpose.
  93. The correct answer is B (A locked-in retirement account holding transferred pension plan funds): A locked-in retirement account holds transferred pension money under preservation rules until it is converted into a qualifying income vehicle. A TFSA is an unrestricted personal savings account, and a deferred profit sharing plan is funded through employer profits. A registered retirement income fund is an income-paying vehicle rather than the usual accumulation account for the transfer described. The dedicated locked-in structure preserves the pension source and restrictions.
  94. The correct answer is D (A life income fund or another locked-in income vehicle preserving pension restrictions): A life income fund converts locked-in retirement savings into regulated income while maintaining pension limits on withdrawals. A group RRSP is an accumulation plan rather than the required locked-in income vehicle. An ordinary RRIF would not preserve the original pension restrictions, and a TFSA cannot receive the locked-in transfer without removing those protections. The applicable pension law determines the exact age and available income vehicle.
  95. The correct answer is C (It reduces her RRSP contribution room to reflect pension benefits already accruing): The pension adjustment measures the value of registered pension benefits earned in a year and lowers the following year's RRSP deduction room. It coordinates two tax-assisted retirement savings systems rather than increasing room. OAS recovery and CPP retirement benefits are separate calculations that a pension adjustment does not directly determine. The employee should account for the adjustment when planning personal RRSP contributions.
  96. The correct answer is D (Defined benefit income is generally predictable while defined contribution income can vary): A defined benefit promise is calculated under a pension formula and is therefore more predictable, while a defined contribution balance and its withdrawals depend on contributions and investment performance. Treating both structures as fixed, assuming defined contribution always pays more, or ending defined benefit income at departure each misstate the plans. Actual amounts still depend on the specific promise, account balance, and payout choices. Predictability is the key distinction for multi-year cash flow planning.
  97. The correct answer is A (A group registered retirement savings plan administered through the employer): A group RRSP is a set of individual RRSP accounts administered through the workplace, often with payroll deductions and possible employer matching. It does not promise a formula benefit like a defined benefit pension. A disability savings plan serves a beneficiary with qualifying disability needs, and a locked-in retirement account holds pension transfers. The individual ownership model distinguishes the group RRSP from a formal pension formula.
  98. The correct answer is B (A deferred profit sharing plan funded from company profit sharing): A deferred profit sharing plan allows employer profit-based contributions to individual employee accounts, with tax deferral until withdrawal. The contribution pattern follows company profitability rather than a fixed pension formula. OAS, TFSAs, and RESPs serve retirement support, personal savings, or education goals rather than employer profit sharing. The profit-linked employer contribution identifies the deferred profit sharing plan.
  99. The correct answer is B (It reduces tax withholding each pay period rather than producing the full benefit at filing): Payroll deduction can lower the tax withheld throughout the year, giving the employee the cash-flow benefit as contributions are made. It does not fix investment returns or convert a group RRSP into a defined benefit pension. Administrative payroll approval also does not remove the need for RRSP deduction room. The employer and plan administrator should apply withholding based on the contribution arrangement and the employee's information.
  100. The correct answer is A (Group RRSP funds generally remain accessible while locked-in pension plan funds face withdrawal restrictions): A group RRSP is made of individual RRSP accounts and ordinarily retains RRSP access rules, while vested pension benefits can become subject to locking-in. Treating both as locked-in, reversing the access rules, or saying neither plan has restrictions ignores that important distinction. Employer matching does not turn a group RRSP into a registered pension plan. The plan documents and applicable pension law should still be reviewed before a transfer.
  101. The correct answer is C (A jointly sponsored multi-employer pension plan pooling several employers): A multi-employer pension plan lets several participating employers sponsor one shared arrangement while employees move among them.
