The correct answer is D (A prescribed annuity can level the taxable interest included in each payment): Prescribed annuity taxation can keep the taxable interest component comparatively stable from one payment to the next. Accrual taxation does not produce equal taxable interest across the schedule and generally concentrates more tax earlier. The full annuity instalment is not treated as a capital gain under the relevant annuity tax rules. Purchasing a non-registered annuity with savings does not make registered treatment apply automatically.
  184. The correct answer is C (Prescribed taxation spreads a stable taxable interest component across payments): A prescribed annuity can level the taxable interest included in each payment and avoid the front-loaded pattern. Accrual taxation produces a larger taxable interest share in the early years, which conflicts with the objective. Capital gains treatment does not make every annuity instalment fully taxable as a capital gain. A non-registered annuity does not receive registered treatment unless the governing registration requirements are met.
  185. The correct answer is A (A prescribed annuity levels the taxable portion while preserving predictable taxation): Prescribed annuity taxation can level the taxable interest portion and give the client the more predictable pattern requested. Accrual taxation does the opposite by concentrating taxable interest in the early payment years. The full instalment is not taxed as a capital gain under ordinary annuity treatment. Using non-registered savings does not trigger registered treatment, so the applicable tax method must be chosen explicitly.
  186. The correct answer is B (A mutual fund matches the growth objective, long horizon, and risk tolerance): A mutual fund fits the stated growth objective because the client accepts volatility and does not require guarantee features. A life annuity converts the capital into income and removes the flexibility needed for long term growth. A term certain annuity also pays fixed income and does not meet the stated growth priority. A segregated guarantee provides protection at specified events but does not eliminate market volatility.
  187. The correct answer is C (A mutual fund fits the long horizon and stated acceptance of market volatility): A mutual fund aligns with the thirty year growth horizon and the client's comfort with market movement. A term certain annuity is built for fixed income over a stated period rather than growth. A life annuity converts the savings into lifetime income and does not fit the requested growth use. A guaranteed segregated fund still exposes the investment account to market movement and adds protection costs she does not need.
  188. The correct answer is D (Mutual fund growth matches the horizon, volatility tolerance, and guarantee preference): A mutual fund is suitable because he wants growth, accepts volatility, and does not need a maturity or death guarantee. A life annuity would convert the capital into income and eliminate the long term growth objective. A term certain annuity provides planned fixed payments rather than the requested growth exposure. A segregated fund guarantee does not abolish market risk and would add insurance features he does not require.
  189. The correct answer is D (An unprotected mutual fund suits the growth goal and thirty year horizon): A mutual fund fits the thirty year growth horizon and avoids paying for guarantees she says she does not need. A life annuity converts the money into income rather than preserving long term growth potential. A term certain annuity imposes fixed payments and does not suit the stated growth objective. A segregated fund guarantee may protect a stated outcome but does not eliminate downside during the investment period.
  190. The correct answer is C (A mutual fund suits growth, volatility tolerance, and the long investment horizon): A mutual fund supports the growth objective because the client accepts volatility and does not require guarantee features. An immediate life annuity converts the capital into income and removes the growth and liquidity profile he wants. A term certain annuity pays fixed income over a chosen period and does not focus on long term growth. A fully guaranteed segregated contract still has investment market risk and adds protection costs beyond his stated needs.
  191. The correct answer is A (The higher fee helps support the segregated fund's maturity and death guarantees): The higher management expense ratio generally reflects the insurance guarantees that the segregated contract provides. Advertising can be a cost, but it is not the principal explanation for the guarantee-related fee difference. Trailing commissions may affect both products and do not account for the entire additional charge. The insurer does not ordinarily refund the added guarantee cost merely because the fund underperforms.
  192. The correct answer is B (Extra management costs help fund the insurance guarantees in the contract): The extra expense pays for the maturity and death benefit protection embedded in the segregated contract. The fee difference does not arise because the segregated fund necessarily trades its underlying securities more often. Including mutual fund trailing commissions does not erase the cost of the insurance guarantees. The insurer does not reverse the additional charge simply because investment returns trail a benchmark.
  193. The correct answer is C (The additional charge funds the insurance guarantees embedded in the contract): The additional management expense ratio supports the segregated contract's contractual guarantees. Marketing and distribution costs may vary, but they do not by themselves explain the complete fee gap. The mutual fund's trailing commissions cannot remove the insurance cost that the segregated contract carries. A weak return year does not normally trigger a refund of the extra management charge.
  194. The correct answer is D (The guarantee features create insurance costs included in the higher management fee): The higher charge includes the cost of providing the maturity and death benefit guarantees. The difference does not arise chiefly from more trading within the segregated fund. Trailing commissions can affect cost comparisons, but they do not eliminate the guarantee charge. The insurer does not automatically return the fee after a single year of poor returns.
  195. The correct answer is A (Suitability weighs stated risk tolerance together with actual capacity for loss): Suitability requires balancing the stated risk preference with the client's real ability to absorb a loss. Risk tolerance alone omits the short retirement horizon and the importance of the entire investment. The near retirement date cannot be ignored because a major decline could affect income immediately. The KYC discussion should record the true situation and not push the client toward a selected product.
  196. The correct answer is B (The advisor should balance risk preference with capacity and time horizon): The advisor must consider the stated risk preference together with the near retirement date and reliance on the investment. A high tolerance answer does not override the imminent need for the capital and the severe effect of a loss. Actual capacity for loss matters because the client cannot treat this investment as expendable risk capital. Changing the KYC answers to fit an aggressive product would reverse the proper suitability process.
  197. The correct answer is A (Financial capacity and the brief horizon must temper the stated risk preference): The near retirement horizon and dependence on the full amount require the advisor to moderate the stated risk preference. A high tolerance response alone does not justify an aggressive product when the client cannot absorb the loss. The short remaining period matters because there is little time to recover before the money is needed. The KYC record should not be altered after product selection to make an unsuitable recommendation appear consistent.
  198. The correct answer is C (The short horizon and weak loss capacity outweigh the stated risk appetite): The advisor must give substantial weight to the one year horizon and the client's reliance on the entire investment. A stated comfort with volatility does not make the full amount expendable when retirement income depends on it. Risk capacity remains relevant because a severe loss would be difficult to recover before income begins. The KYC record should be completed honestly before choosing a product rather than revised afterward.
  199. The correct answer is D (Confirm irregular cash needs because immediate annuitization greatly limits liquidity): The advisor must confirm the need for irregular withdrawals because annuitized capital is generally not available for those costs. The income guarantee addresses longevity but does not provide liquidity for sudden medical or family expenses. An immediate life annuity normally cannot be surrendered for the full original deposit. A desire for some growth protection does not replace the need to assess short term access and suitability.
  200. The correct answer is B (Confirm the liquidity requirement before annuitizing most of the available capital): The client's irregular medical and family costs require the advisor to confirm both liquidity needs and the amount that can safely be annuitized. A guaranteed lifetime income does not create cash for unexpected expenses that may arise soon. An immediate life annuity generally sacrifices the original capital and does not provide a full refund right. A preference for some growth protection does not make full immediate annuitization suitable without further analysis.
  201. The correct answer is C (Near-term liquidity needs merit review because an immediate life annuity generally has restricted cash surrender access.): An immediate life annuity exchanges capital for scheduled payments, so access to the original principal is usually restricted. Treating guaranteed income as superior ignores the client's stated need for irregular withdrawals. Assuming a full surrender right misstates the usual liquidity feature of a life annuity. Guaranteed growth protection does not replace a cash reserve for unexpected expenses.
  202. The correct answer is D (The advisor should first confirm near-term liquidity needs because annuitized capital generally cannot be surrendered.): The client's plan requires a separate reserve before any capital is exchanged for guaranteed annuity income. Prioritizing income overlooks the announced medical and family expenses that require flexible access. A complete surrender right does not match the usual irreversible nature of an immediate life annuity. Growth guarantees cannot serve as an emergency fund when the underlying capital is committed to scheduled payments.
  203. The correct answer is B (The contributing spouse deducts the contribution; withdrawals usually belong to the annuitant, subject to attribution.): The contributing spouse claims the deduction, subject to available contribution room. The annuitant generally reports withdrawals, but withdrawals in the contribution year or next two calendar years may be attributed to the contributor under the spousal RRSP attribution rule. Legal ownership does not transfer the deduction to the annuitant. Neither spouse automatically divides the deduction according to marginal tax rates.
  204. The correct answer is A (The RRSP must mature by December 31 of the year the holder reaches age seventy-one.): Canadian tax rules require an RRSP to mature by December 31 of the year its owner reaches age seventy-one. Continuing the RRSP beyond that deadline would contravene the maturity rule and create adverse tax consequences. Retirement by itself does not dictate the conversion date because the age rule governs the plan. Earned income affects contribution room, while the holder's age determines when the RRSP must mature.
  205. The correct answer is C (A RRIF requires the prescribed minimum withdrawal each year beginning in the year after opening.): RRIF minimums are mandatory after the first year and are calculated from the account value and the holder's age. Postponing the prescribed amount would not comply with the RRIF withdrawal rules. Other retirement income does not make the annual minimum optional under the plan. The requirement is not limited to younger holders and continues throughout the RRIF's life.
  206. The correct answer is A (Regular RRIFs have no maximum withdrawal limit, although a prescribed minimum still applies annually.): A regular RRIF permits withdrawals above the minimum, so the client can obtain additional cash for the renovation. Treating the prescribed minimum as a cap confuses a required floor with an upper limit. Insurer approval is not the mechanism that controls excess RRIF withdrawals. A fixed age-based maximum does not apply because regular RRIFs do not impose that ceiling.
  207. The correct answer is B (A qualifying spousal rollover can defer tax by moving the proceeds into her own registered plan.): A qualifying spouse can generally transfer inherited RRIF proceeds into her own RRSP or RRIF without immediate tax. Retirement status is not the condition that creates the spousal rollover opportunity. A qualifying surviving spouse is not automatically required to report the full RRIF value as immediate income. Taking the money in cash first would defeat the tax-deferred continuity that the rollover can provide.
  208. The correct answer is D (Unused TFSA contribution room accumulates and remains available for future years without expiry.): TFSA room that is not used carries forward and adds to room available in later years. Annual expiry does not apply because unused TFSA space is cumulative. The carry-forward period is not limited to three calendar years. Reaching age sixty-five does not cause a TFSA holder to forfeit accumulated contribution room.
  209. The correct answer is D (Withdrawn amounts return to contribution room on January 1 of the following calendar year.): A TFSA withdrawal restores contribution room in the next calendar year, not on the withdrawal date. Immediate same-year replacement can cause an over-contribution when no other room remains. Withdrawn amounts are not permanently lost because they are restored in the following year. The rule uses the next calendar year rather than a fixed ninety-day waiting period.
  210. The correct answer is B (A Tax-Free Savings Account can provide tax-free growth and withdrawals that do not affect income-tested benefits.): A TFSA fits the client's goals because both growth and withdrawals receive tax-free treatment. A RRIF produces taxable income and does not preserve income-tested benefits in the manner described. A LIRA receives locked-in pension money and does not accept voluntary deposits like the account requested. RRSP withdrawals are taxable and can increase income used in benefit tests.
  211. The correct answer is C (A Registered Education Savings Plan can combine education saving with eligible federal grant programs.): An RESP is designed for post-secondary saving and can attract grants such as the Canada Education Savings Grant. A Registered Disability Savings Plan addresses long-term disability needs rather than education funding. A TFSA does not provide dedicated education grants for a beneficiary. A Registered Retirement Savings Plan serves retirement saving and does not supply education grants.
  212. The correct answer is A (The family can receive an accumulated income payment subject to tax and eligibility conditions.): An accumulated income payment can release RESP investment income when the beneficiary does not pursue eligible education. The income does not become government property because specific payment options remain available. It is not withdrawn tax-free, since accumulated income is generally taxable and may face an additional levy. The RESP does not freeze permanently, although plan age limits and transfer or beneficiary-change rules affect the available choices.
  213. The correct answer is C (A Registered Disability Savings Plan can combine disability grants and bonds with tax-deferred growth.): An RDSP supports a beneficiary who qualifies for the Disability Tax Credit and can receive Canada Disability Savings Grants and Bonds. An RESP provides education grants and does not target long-term disability support. A TFSA provides tax-free saving but not disability grant entitlements. A non-registered account lacks the RDSP's tax deferral and government grant structure.
  214. The correct answer is B (Lifetime Disability Assistance Payments are recurring RDSP withdrawals that continue at least annually.): A Lifetime Disability Assistance Payment is the recurring RDSP payment that must continue annually once started. Locked-in Life Income Payments belong to pension income vehicles rather than RDSP contracts. Accumulated Income Payments are associated with education savings plans. Registered Minimum Withdrawal Payments are not the recognized RDSP term for these continuing disability payments.
  215. The correct answer is D (A qualifying rollover can move the deceased parent's RRSP proceeds into the child's RDSP.): Federal tax rules can permit an RRSP rollover to an RDSP for a financially dependent child who qualifies for the Disability Tax Credit. An RDSP is eligible to receive such proceeds when the statutory dependency and disability conditions are met. Routing the money through a TFSA is not a required step and could create separate tax and contribution issues. Both RRSP and RRIF proceeds can qualify for this dependent-child rollover.
  216. The correct answer is A (A Locked-in Retirement Account receives transferred pension funds while preserving withdrawal restrictions.): A LIRA is designed for locked-in pension money and keeps the funds subject to pension legislation. A TFSA cannot preserve pension locking-in status for transferred funds. An RESP serves education saving and is not the designated destination for a pension transfer. A group RRSP generally does not preserve the restrictive locked-in treatment that applies to these funds.
  217. The correct answer is C (A LIRA typically converts into a life income fund or another pension-approved income vehicle.): A LIRA cannot pay retirement income directly, so conversion to a LIF or comparable vehicle allows pension-compliant withdrawals. A TFSA does not preserve the legislative locking-in requirements attached to LIRA assets. A full cash withdrawal is not generally available because the pension rules restrict access. A LIRA is not designed to remain indefinitely without an income option, since conversion permits retirement payments.
  218. The correct answer is A (A LIF generally imposes both minimum and maximum annual withdrawals due to pension locking-in.): LIF assets retain pension restrictions, which produce an annual floor and ceiling around retirement income withdrawals. A LIF does not behave like an unlimited regular RRIF because pension legislation limits access. The maximum does not replace the minimum, since the holder must still take the prescribed annual amount. These limits are not deferred until age eighty and generally apply once withdrawals begin.
  219. The correct answer is D (Many jurisdictions permit small locked-in balances to be unlocked under prescribed threshold rules.): Many pension laws allow a small LIRA to be unlocked when its value falls below a jurisdiction-specific threshold. Unlocking is not universally automatic because the amount and conditions depend on the governing legislation. A job change does not by itself release every locked-in dollar from the former plan. Small balance provisions are not restricted to holders age eighty-five or older.
  220. The correct answer is B (Many pension jurisdictions allow hardship unlocking of locked-in funds with supporting documents.): Pension legislation in many jurisdictions provides a hardship process that can release some locked-in money after documented medical or financial need. Locked-in funds are not inaccessible everywhere because jurisdiction-specific exceptions can exist. The process can extend to former members with LIRA assets rather than only active plan members. Hardship relief does not require surrendering the entire LIRA balance and may release only an approved amount.
  221. The correct answer is C (A defined benefit plan promises a formula-based benefit while defined contribution defines only contributions.): A defined benefit promise uses a benefit formula, whereas a defined contribution design specifies deposits but not the final income. A defined contribution plan does not guarantee a monthly pension based on final salary. The two designs do not produce identical guaranteed income because their promises differ. A defined benefit plan is not limited to returning contributions, since its purpose is to fund a formula benefit.
  222. The correct answer is A (Vesting determines when employer contributions become the employee's non-forfeitable property.): Vesting marks the point when the employee gains an unconditional right to employer contributions under the plan. Locking-in governs withdrawal restrictions for pension money rather than the ownership milestone itself. Indexing adjusts pension payments for inflation and does not establish contribution ownership. Portability controls movement of vested funds between eligible plans rather than the vesting event.
  223. The correct answer is D (Portability allows a vested member to transfer the entitlement into another eligible vehicle.): Portability describes the ability to move a vested pension entitlement into another permitted plan such as a LIRA. Indexing concerns inflation protection rather than moving pension capital. Vesting establishes ownership of employer contributions but does not itself authorize the transfer. Benefit coordination integrates pension income with government benefits and does not create transfer rights.
  224. The correct answer is B (The employee bears investment risk because the final benefit depends on account performance.): A defined contribution account converts contributions and investment results into retirement capital, so poor returns reduce the member's eventual balance. The employer does not absorb every loss because this design does not promise a fixed benefit. No government guarantee supplies a minimum return for a defined contribution account. Risk is not automatically split equally, since these plan designs allocate it differently.
  225. The correct answer is B (Many plans provide a reduced ongoing pension to a surviving spouse after the member dies.): A survivor benefit commonly continues pension income at a reduced percentage, with the exact formula set by the plan and applicable pension law. Group pension plans can provide ongoing income to an eligible spouse. The survivor does not repay the member's past pension payments. Survivor benefits can be paid periodically instead of being limited to one lump sum.
  226. The correct answer is C (A Registered Retirement Savings Plan accepts eligible earned-income contributions and provides tax-deferred growth.): An RRSP suits the self-employed client because earned income can create deduction room for voluntary contributions. A LIRA receives locked-in pension transfers rather than ordinary new deposits from earned income. A LIF is an income vehicle for locked-in money and does not accept new contributions. A group pension plan requires an employer-sponsored arrangement rather than an individual self-employed plan.
  227. The correct answer is D (A named RRIF beneficiary can allow proceeds to bypass the estate and probate process.): A valid RRIF beneficiary designation can move proceeds directly to the beneficiary rather than through the estate. The designation can affect both how probate applies and how the funds are distributed. Claiming that a designation never affects probate ignores the direct beneficiary route. A direct payout is intended to avoid mandatory estate administration, subject to the governing jurisdiction and contract.
  228. The correct answer is A (The deceased's final return generally includes the RRSP value, with tax ordinarily paid by the estate.): The RRSP value is generally included in the deceased owner's income, and the resulting liability is usually met from the estate when no qualifying rollover applies. The named adult child receives the proceeds but does not ordinarily report the full value personally in this scenario. A non-spouse designation does not remove the default tax treatment. A valid beneficiary designation changes distribution and may avoid probate, but it does not erase the income tax liability.
  229. The correct answer is B (An RRSP deduction reduces current taxable income, and plan growth is generally tax-deferred.): An eligible RRSP contribution creates a deduction that lowers taxable income while investment income can remain sheltered until withdrawal. The contribution is not limited to deferring investment tax without any current deduction. Growth inside the RRSP is not the deductible amount because the contribution supports the deduction. The plan does not tax the contribution itself before it can reduce current taxable income.
  230. The correct answer is D (A qualifying home buyer withdrawal allows temporary tax-free access and later RRSP repayment.): The Home Buyers' Plan can provide temporary tax-free access to eligible RRSP funds, followed by scheduled repayment. The withdrawal is not permanently tax-free because an unrepaid amount can become taxable. Ordinary RRSP taxation does not apply when the Home Buyers' Plan conditions are met and repayment is handled correctly. The plan can generally be used by the eligible buyer's own RRSP rather than being restricted to a spouse's account.
  231. The correct answer is A (A qualifying lifelong learning withdrawal permits temporary tax-free access and scheduled repayment.): The Lifelong Learning Plan can allow eligible RRSP funds to support qualifying education without immediate tax when the plan conditions are met. The withdrawal is not treated as ordinary fully taxable income at the outset. The eligible holder can generally use their own RRSP rather than being restricted to a spouse's account. Repayment remains required because an unpaid annual portion can become taxable income.
  232. The correct answer is C (A TFSA can be advantageous when future withdrawals will face a higher tax rate than today.): TFSA withdrawals are tax-free, so paying tax now can be preferable when the client expects a higher future marginal rate. An RRSP deduction is more valuable when the contribution rate exceeds the withdrawal rate, which reverses this client's expected pattern. Comparing contribution and withdrawal rates is central to choosing between the two accounts. Employment and RRSP participation do not make TFSA saving unavailable.
  233. The correct answer is A (A non-resident gains no new TFSA room and may face a penalty for new contributions.): A TFSA holder does not accumulate room while non-resident, and a contribution during that period can attract a monthly penalty tax. Leaving Canada does not create extra room for the years spent abroad. The existing TFSA can generally remain open rather than requiring immediate closure. New non-resident contributions are restricted rather than being free of penalty.
  234. The correct answer is C (Employer-sponsored group RRSP contributions can reduce income tax withheld through payroll.): Payroll can reflect group RRSP deductions when the arrangement has the required withholding approval, providing the tax benefit during the year. The contribution is not limited to a year-end withholding adjustment. The employee retains the tax deferral and deduction treatment rather than the employer receiving the entire benefit. A group RRSP contribution is not taxed at deposit and again at withdrawal in the manner described.
  235. The correct answer is B (Contributing enough to obtain the full employer match captures additional tax-deferred retirement savings.): An employer match adds retirement money because the employee receives employer contributions in addition to personal deposits. Employer group RRSP contributions are generally reported as a taxable benefit, while a corresponding RRSP deduction can offset the amount when eligible room exists. The match directly increases retirement saving when the employee reaches the plan's threshold. Matching arrangements can exist in group RRSPs and are not confined to defined benefit plans.
  236. The correct answer is D (Locking-in applies once either employer or employee money enters a pension plan.): Pension legislation can lock both employee and employer contributions once they enter a registered pension plan. Source of contribution does not exempt the employee's payroll deposits from the pension rules. A personal RRSP is not the governing structure for these locked-in deposits. Employee pension contributions are not generally available as unrestricted cash at any age.
  237. The correct answer is B (The jurisdiction that originally governed the pension plan continues to control the LIRA rules.): Transferred locked-in funds generally remain subject to the pension legislation that governed the original plan. A change of residence does not necessarily replace that governing jurisdiction. Federal rules apply only when the original plan was federally regulated rather than automatically to every LIRA. The client cannot freely select whichever jurisdiction provides the most flexible withdrawal provisions.
  238. The correct answer is A (Locked-in LIRA funds must use a LIF or comparable vehicle instead of merging with RRSP money.): LIRA assets remain governed by pension locking-in rules and cannot be freely combined with an ordinary RRIF funded from the RRSP. Combining freely would erase the distinction between locked and unlocked money. The LIRA cannot stay open indefinitely without an eventual income-conversion framework, while the RRSP follows its own maturity path. Age below fifty-five does not create a consolidation right between locked and unlocked accounts.
  239. The correct answer is C (Some jurisdictions allow a LIF to convert to a life annuity as the holder ages.): Certain pension regimes allow conversion options or additional flexibility at defined later ages. A LIF is not necessarily limited to one forced lump-sum distribution. Establishing the account does not remove every later planning choice, although the governing statute controls the menu. LIF rules vary by jurisdiction rather than being identical across Canada.
  240. The correct answer is D (A defined benefit plan generally places funding and investment risk primarily on the employer.): A defined benefit promise requires the employer to fund the benefit formula and address investment or longevity shortfalls. A defined contribution plan generally shifts investment risk to the employee after contributions are made. Both designs can involve employer funding, so the premise that neither assigns responsibility is incorrect. Defined benefit and defined contribution plans do not divide risk in an equal fixed proportion.
  241. The correct answer is D (Family RESP funds can often be shared among eligible siblings, subject to grant limits.): A family RESP can support multiple eligible siblings, and available funds can often help another beneficiary when one child does not pursue study. Contributions are not necessarily locked to one child, although grant and lifetime limits require careful tracking. One beneficiary's decision not to attend school does not automatically terminate the entire family plan. The arrangement exists to benefit named students rather than being reserved solely for the subscriber.
  242. The correct answer is B (The subscriber retains control over RESP investments and withdrawal timing.): The subscriber generally remains the plan holder and directs the RESP, even after the beneficiary becomes an adult. Reaching age eighteen does not by itself transfer control to the beneficiary. A financial institution administers the plan but does not replace the subscriber as the decision-maker. Court appointment is not a routine requirement for RESP investment and withdrawal decisions.
  243. The correct answer is C (Disability Tax Credit eligibility is required for the beneficiary to open and maintain an RDSP.): RDSP eligibility depends on the beneficiary qualifying for the Disability Tax Credit, so this is a statutory condition rather than optional guidance. A Canadian resident cannot establish an RDSP for any beneficiary without the required disability eligibility. The credit is not merely a recommended planning feature because it determines plan eligibility. The requirement can apply to adult beneficiaries and is not limited to children under eighteen.
  244. The correct answer is A (Recent grant and bond amounts may require proportional repayment when an RDSP withdrawal is made.): The ten-year repayment rule can require repayment of recent grant and bond amounts in proportion to an RDSP withdrawal. An RDSP withdrawal can therefore trigger a government repayment rather than always being free of that consequence. Personal contributions are not the amounts subject to this particular clawback. The rule is based on the timing of withdrawal and government assistance rather than a minimum beneficiary age of sixty-five.
  245. The correct answer is B (RRIF income generally qualifies for pension income splitting once the holder reaches sixty-five.): At age sixty-five and later, RRIF payments can generally count as eligible pension income for spousal allocation. This treatment supports splitting rather than preventing it after the qualifying age. Ordinary RRSP withdrawals do not receive the same pension income splitting treatment. A defined benefit pension is not required because eligible RRIF income can also qualify.
  246. The correct answer is A (Membership in a registered pension plan typically reduces RRSP room through a pension adjustment.): A pension adjustment reports the value of registered pension benefits accrued for the year and reduces the next year's RRSP deduction room. Pension membership therefore affects room rather than leaving it unchanged. The adjustment can reduce room instead of increasing it for every plan member. Both defined benefit and defined contribution plans can generate pension adjustments.
  247. The correct answer is C (Early conversion can support pension credit or income splitting planning after age sixty-five.): RRIF income can become eligible pension income at age sixty-five, so an earlier conversion may help position the client for pension credit or splitting strategies. Conversion before seventy-one is permitted generally, although the tradeoffs require analysis. Government benefit collection is not the trigger that permits conversion. A RRIF remains subject to minimum withdrawals, so early conversion does not eliminate that obligation.
  248. The correct answer is D (Segregated contracts with an eligible beneficiary may provide creditor protection governed by provincial insurance law.): Segregated fund contracts may offer creditor protection when a valid beneficiary designation and the provincial insurance rules support that outcome. The insurance contract structure can create protection that an ordinary mutual fund may lack. Provincial law does not universally deny creditor protection to registered investments. Non-registered contracts are not the only segregated contracts that may qualify under insurance legislation.
  249. The correct answer is D (Segregated contracts can guarantee a stated minimum percentage of eligible deposits at maturity.): A segregated contract can provide a maturity guarantee calculated from eligible deposits when the stated maturity conditions are met. The guarantee can apply within registered plans rather than being limited to non-registered contracts. Holding the contract inside an RRSP does not itself terminate the insurance guarantee. The benefit addresses market losses at the guarantee date rather than acting as protection only for client withdrawals.
  250. The correct answer is A (Successor annuitant status is generally limited to a qualifying spouse or common-law partner.): The successor annuitant role on a RRIF generally allows a qualifying spouse or common-law partner to continue the contract, not an independent adult grandchild. Relationship and dependency conditions therefore matter when choosing the estate-planning role. RRIF contracts can support successor annuitant arrangements rather than excluding them. The role concerns continuation of the registered contract and does not make the successor responsible for the deceased's personal debts.

Practice Test 4 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is B (A direct transfer to the dependent child's registered plan may defer tax.): An RRIF transfer to the financially dependent adult child's RRSP, RRIF, or RDSP can defer tax when the statutory dependency and impairment conditions are met. Immediate estate taxation overlooks that eligible dependent exception. A spouse rollover addresses a different survivor category. A minor child's treatment depends on the applicable dependent-child rules rather than automatic immediate taxation.
  2. The correct answer is C (Taking commuted value shifts investment and longevity risk to the client.): Taking the commuted value moves investment performance and longevity risk to the client while giving personal control over the funds. Remaining in the defined benefit plan generally leaves those risks with the plan and preserves the promised lifetime pension. Strong returns do not guarantee that the commuted value will produce more lifetime income. The alternatives do not allocate risk in the same way.
  3. The correct answer is A (Disclose the compensation difference before the client decides which contract to buy.): Disclosure before the purchase allows the client to judge whether the compensation difference influenced the advice. Silence and automatic substitution both prevent the client from making that comparison. An inaccurate claim of identical commissions also misstates a material conflict. The alternative reflects a distinct error: An unexplained switch does not provide adequate disclosure.
  4. The correct answer is D (Disclose the referral fee and family relationship before any annuity recommendation.): A referral fee involving a sibling can reasonably affect how a client views the advice. Earlier disclosure lets the client assess that conflict before a purchase decision. Hiding the arrangement, disclosing only on request, or waiting until afterward all prevent timely informed consent. The alternative reflects a distinct error: Post-purchase disclosure arrives too late for informed choice.
  5. The correct answer is B (Explain the underwriting relevance and record information needed for an accurate application.): Explaining the underwriting purpose lets the client understand why the omitted facts matter without ignoring privacy concerns. The agent should record the information required for a complete and accurate submission. Silent omission, an undisclosed file entry, and an all or nothing refusal each fail to balance those duties. The alternative reflects a distinct error: Silent omission removes information needed for underwriting.
  6. The correct answer is C (Explain that falsifying the application date is prohibited and decline the request.): The signed application date must reflect the true completion date, and a request to alter it creates a false record. A client disclaimer does not authorize false information in an application. Legitimate age saving, where an insurer permits a backdated policy effective date, follows insurer underwriting rules and does not involve falsifying the signed application. The alternative reflects a distinct error: Partial backdating still creates a material misrepresentation.
  7. The correct answer is C (Base the recommendation on suitability and place the client's interests first.): A suitability analysis compares the products with the client's documented objectives, needs, and circumstances. Compensation becomes a conflict that must not steer the decision when a better fit exists. Higher pay, insurer convenience, and an uninformed client selection all displace that professional judgment. The alternative reflects a distinct error: Higher compensation cannot determine an unsuitable recommendation.
  8.   54. The correct answer is D (Policy gain of fifteen thousand dollars after reducing proceeds by the loan): On a full surrender, proceeds of disposition equal the gross cash surrender value less the debt repaid from it. The 10,000-dollar policy loan therefore reduces the tax proceeds to 50,000 dollars, and the policy gain is 50,000 minus 35,000, or 15,000 dollars. A policy gain is included in ordinary income rather than taxed as a capital gain. The result reflects the net amount available from the contract and the adjusted cost basis described in the stem.

Prescribed annuities do not make every payment tax free, and non-prescribed contracts do not report the entire payment as taxable income. The comparison concerns the timing and allocation of taxable interest between the two contract forms.

A loan can therefore defer tax on value not withdrawn, but the loan itself is not tax neutral. Critical illness provides no general exemption from these life insurance policy rules.

  76.   77. The older whole life contract has accumulated more basis, leaving a smaller gap at surrender. The aggressively funded universal life contract has less basis relative to its cash value, which usually creates the larger gain.   78.   80.   81. Neither layer is automatically tax free on disposition, and personal premium deductibility is not the reason for the structure.
  82. The correct answer is B (Zero adjusted cost basis makes full cash surrender value a taxable policy gain): Adjusted cost basis can fall through the annual net cost of pure insurance even while level premiums continue. If basis has reached zero, cash surrender value minus zero leaves the full amount as a policy gain. Premium history does not prevent this reduction, and the gain must still be calculated at surrender. A zero basis therefore increases rather than removes the tax consequence of receiving cash value.
  83. The correct answer is B (Named life insurance beneficiaries usually receive proceeds outside the probate process): A properly named life insurance beneficiary generally receives proceeds directly without waiting for probate. A standard individually owned non-registered mutual fund account generally lacks a direct beneficiary designation and passes under estate rules. Joint ownership or a registered plan can produce different results, so provincial law and account terms matter. The usual distinction is that the insurance designation can avoid probate delay and fees.
  84. The correct answer is C (Direct trust designation generally allows faster proceeds because probate is bypassed): A trust named directly as policy beneficiary can receive proceeds without the policy passing through the estate first. That structure can reduce estate-administration delay while preserving trustee control over distributions. Funds routed through the general estate must normally wait for estate settlement before the trust is funded. A direct designation is not slower by definition, and estate administration does not provide an identical timeline.
  85. The correct answer is D (A direct spouse designation can avoid probate and improve creditor protection): A direct spouse beneficiary generally keeps the death benefit outside the estate, reducing probate exposure. An eligible spouse designation may also receive stronger creditor protection under provincial insurance law. An estate designation places proceeds among estate assets subject to administration and claims. The protection is not identical in every province, so the advisor should confirm the beneficiary eligibility and local rules before relying on it.
  86. The correct answer is A (Both payouts reach the beneficiary tax free, but a non-exempt policy can create a final gain): The named beneficiary generally receives either death benefit without including the payout in income. Exempt status controls tax inside the contract and at the policyholder level. A non-exempt policy is deemed to be disposed of immediately before death, so a final policy gain above adjusted cost basis can be taxable to the owner or estate. Annual accrual tax already reported on the non-exempt policy reduces the remaining gain but does not make the two tax structures identical.
  87. The correct answer is D (The converted policy follows permanent policy tax rules using its own adjusted cost basis): Once converted to permanent coverage, the contract is taxed as a permanent policy with its own adjusted cost basis. Future cash value, exempt-test limits, and any policy gain follow the resulting contract rather than a special conversion exemption. The conversion privilege helps access permanent insurance but does not eliminate tax calculations. The original term premium history also does not override the basis established for the converted contract.
  88. The correct answer is C (Long-term deferral may conflict with early access that creates taxable policy gain): Maximum exempt funding offers attractive long-term tax deferral but is less flexible when the client may need money within a short period. An early withdrawal or surrender can create a policy gain when proceeds exceed adjusted cost basis. The taxable alternative should be compared using actual liquidity timing, expected returns, and the client's tax rate. Exempt-test limits apply during the policy's operation and do not wait for ten completed years.
  89. The correct answer is A (The premium is higher than standard rates because mortality risk is increased): A table rating is an underwriting class that adds premium for mortality risk rather than automatically declining the application. The client can generally use the offered policy for both buy-sell funding and personal income replacement at the rated cost. The rating does not make the contract lapse when contestability ends. It also applies according to the insured risk and policy terms, not according to whether the coverage serves a business or personal purpose.
  90. The correct answer is B (Calculate business and personal needs separately before combining their total coverage): The buy-sell amount should reflect the business ownership value, while personal income replacement should reflect the family's separate financial need. Calculating both purposes independently prevents one need from being mistaken for the other before the amounts are combined for underwriting. A recent cardiac stent may lead to a rating or additional evidence but does not automatically make the partner uninsurable. Insuring only the healthier owner or dropping family protection would leave recognized exposures unfunded.
  91. The correct answer is B (Key person amounts are justified through company financials rather than personal income): Key person coverage protects the business against the insured person's economic contribution, so financial underwriting examines company revenue, replacement cost, and related business evidence. Personal mortgage coverage is justified through the individual's income, debts, and overall financial needs. The same insured life can therefore support two different face amounts and evidence requirements. Mortgage protection still requires health underwriting, and personal coverage is not automatically larger than every business policy.

A cross-purchase structure often requires each owner to acquire and coordinate coverage on the other owners, creating more documents as the ownership group changes. Premium cost depends on underwriting and coverage, not structure alone, and cross-purchase still requires underwriting. Standard classification is also based on risk rather than the ownership arrangement.

The arrangement does not transfer full ownership or permanently replace the policy's beneficiary structure. Because the lender relies on an existing policy, the assignment is not automatically treated as a new insurance application.

The described omission therefore does not void the coverage indefinitely or require a percentage reduction. Premium repayment is not a normal condition for receiving the death benefit under an incontestability clause. Convenience for later reviews does not broaden the purpose for which sensitive information was collected. A service refusal penalizes the client and does not address the privacy obligation.

The correct answer is A (Recommend term coverage matching the mortgage term, despite the lower compensation): A ten year mortgage need is matched by term coverage with a corresponding duration, and compensation must not override suitability. Permanent coverage may serve other goals, but those added benefits fall outside the client's stated obligation. Brochures cannot replace the advisor's explanation of which product fits the documented need. Changing coverage details without disclosure prevents an informed recommendation.
  229. The correct answer is B (Complete replacement disclosure and comparison promptly before finalizing coverage): Urgent timing can justify prompt action, but it does not remove the CISRO replacement declaration and comparison duties. The client needs the comparison before deciding whether to replace existing protection. Completing forms after issue would deprive the client of timely information. The advisor must satisfy applicable disclosure rules instead of relying on underwriting to decide their necessity.
  230. The correct answer is D (Keep the friend's data separate from the sister's independent assessment): A referral does not authorize use of the existing client's confidential information for another person's application. Family ties do not create implied consent for a different underwriting purpose. Another applicant cannot validate a disclosure on behalf of the original client. Indirect references can still reveal personal information and therefore require a proper authority.
  231. The correct answer is A (Report the rebating concern through compliance or the appropriate regulatory channel): Unauthorized rebating undermines fair competition and suitability, and a credible concern should go to compliance or the regulator. Witnessing misconduct is enough to trigger an escalation duty even when the observer did not participate. A private warning does not preserve the records and oversight required for a serious conduct concern. The obligation to report does not depend on waiting for a client to complain.
  232. The correct answer is C (Meet the daughter alone to verify her understanding and independent consent): Speaking with the daughter directly tests whether she understands the coverage and chooses it without pressure. A family member's helpful intent does not establish the applicant's independent knowledge. The applicant remains responsible for her answers even when another person offers assistance. A private discussion can resolve uncertainty, so permanent rejection is unnecessary without other concerns.
  233. The correct answer is D (Base every recommendation on documented client need rather than the reward contest): Suitability requires recommendations supported by each client's documented circumstances, independent of sales incentives. A contest reward cannot justify recommending coverage above the need. Undisclosed influence from an incentive prevents fair treatment and informed choice. Withdrawing service does not address the conflict and may leave legitimate clients without required advice.
  234. The correct answer is B (Decline any referral plan that rewards coverage volume instead of client need): Suitable insurance advice must follow the borrower's needs, while a volume based referral arrangement rewards a conflicting result. Informal cooperation does not make a conflicted incentive acceptable. Directing coverage toward less confident clients exploits vulnerability. Disclosure can explain a conflict, but it cannot repair an arrangement that ties product selection to referral volume.
  235. The correct answer is A (Explain the disclosure requirement and complete the source of funds accurately): Source of funds questions support insurer due diligence and anti-money-laundering controls, so the response must be truthful. A possible family dispute does not permit an incomplete answer. Changing the gift into employment income creates an inaccurate record. The advisor should resolve an incomplete disclosure before submission rather than relying on later detection.
  236. The correct answer is B (Document the affordability concern and recommend a lower amount the client can sustain): Coverage likely to lapse because of premium strain is not suitable even when the client requests the maximum amount. A warning conversation does not remove the advisor's responsibility to recommend an affordable solution. An undisclosed future reduction would contradict the application and the client's instructions. Ending service is unnecessary when documentation and a sustainable alternative can address the issue.
  237. The correct answer is C (Compare both contracts objectively and explain losses from replacing suitable coverage): A replacement decision requires an objective comparison of benefits, costs, guarantees, taxes, and rights that may be lost. The client's stated preference does not remove the advisor's duty to explain a suitable existing contract. Refusing discussion prevents informed choice and is not a proportionate response. Premium matching does not address contestability, cash value, surrender charges, or other replacement effects.
  238. The correct answer is D (Explain the duty and update the insurer with accurate client information): An activity that may affect underwriting must be disclosed accurately, and the insurer should receive the updated information. A known upcoming risk can be material before the first day of participation. Waiting for an injury defeats the purpose of timely underwriting disclosure. An internal note does not inform the insurer that bears the coverage risk.
  239. The correct answer is D (Tell the client about the finding and discuss suitable corrective steps): A known suitability failure requires transparent discussion so the client can understand the issue and choose a correction. Client silence does not erase the advisor's professional duty. A later market recovery would not make the original recommendation suitable. Undisclosed changes deprive the client of the information needed for an informed decision.
  240. The correct answer is C (Use the alternate mailing arrangement and record the client's reason): A reasonable alternate address can protect confidentiality, and documenting the request supports both service and compliance. The household address may be the source of the privacy or safety concern. Contacting the spouse could expose information and defeat the client's protection plan. Policy cancellation is unnecessary when a workable confidential delivery option exists.
  241. The correct answer is A (Process the informed beneficiary choice as the competent client instructed): A competent client's informed beneficiary decision belongs to the client, and the advisor should carry out those instructions accurately. Repeated requests to reconsider substitute the advisor's preference for the client's choice. The children were not named and do not receive authority to participate in the private planning discussion. An undisclosed beneficiary change is a serious unauthorized alteration.
  242. The correct answer is B (Decline the beneficiary designation and explain the advisor's conflict of interest): An advisor named as beneficiary stands to gain from the estate plan and cannot provide ordinary independent advice on that designation. Sincere gratitude does not remove the conflict created by receiving a client benefit. Seeking a larger gift worsens the conflict and exploits the advisory relationship. Confidentiality does not cure the conflict and may prevent required oversight.
  243. The correct answer is D (Decline to sign unless the colleague has completed a genuine personal file review): A suitability signature attests to personal review, so the experienced advisor must see and assess the actual client file. A later review cannot validate a signature that was given before the work occurred. A verbal assurance does not replace the records and analysis required for the attestation. Recruiting another signer compounds the compliance breach rather than resolving it.
  244. The correct answer is B (Use qualified interpretation or translated materials for full understanding): Informed consent requires accurate understanding of the application and its terms, so qualified language support is appropriate. A partial summary can omit conditions that affect the client's decision. A family member's statement does not guarantee complete or neutral interpretation. Changing standardized questions does not replace communication in a language the client understands.
  245. The correct answer is C (Escalate the misleading dividend guidance through compliance or the regulator): Describing non guaranteed dividends as assured income can mislead consumers, and the concern should reach compliance or the regulator. A different reporting line does not remove the duty to escalate known misconduct. Informal discussion does not create the oversight and records required for a serious sales concern. The advisor may report before any complaint if the misleading guidance is already known.
  246. The correct answer is A (Process the cancellation promptly within the free look period): Free look rights let the client reconsider a new policy, so a timely cancellation request should be handled promptly. Commission impact is not a valid reason to resist the client's choice. Administrative delay could extinguish a consumer protection before the request can be honoured. A side payment to preserve the sale conflicts with the client's right to cancel.
  247. The correct answer is C (Discuss the discrepancy and record accurate figures before submission): An advisor who notices a material inconsistency should clarify it with the client and ensure the application reflects accurate information. Client supplied data still requires review when the stated figure conflicts with observable facts. An undisclosed middle estimate would invent information rather than resolve the discrepancy. Leaving a required risk field blank delays underwriting and does not produce an accurate application.
  248. The correct answer is A (Explain renewal pricing fully and honestly even when that may affect the sale): Material facts about renewal pricing must be explained clearly because they affect the policy's long term affordability. Softening the mechanism to protect a sale prevents informed comparison. Changing the subject does not satisfy the disclosure duty. Common client behaviour does not prove that future renewal pricing will be irrelevant to this purchaser.
  249. The correct answer is B (Use only publicly available product information rather than private spouse comments): Client comparisons should rely on verified public product information rather than confidential or unverified material from a spouse's employer. A lack of documents does not make the private comment public. Further confirmation from the spouse would deepen the confidentiality problem. Labelling a rumour does not make an improper disclosure suitable for client advice.
  250. The correct answer is D (Direct the client to insurer approved payment channels that create a reliable record): Premiums should move through approved insurer channels that receipt and record the payment promptly. Personal custody can delay remittance and create uncertainty about whether the policy received the money. A handwritten receipt does not provide the accounting and compliance protections of the insurer's system. The client's comfort level does not make personal handling of a premium payment compliant.
